NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
Fern Benally • Alberto L. Peshlakai •Jason E. Whiting • Daryl Seymore • Dawnafe Whitesinger
“We are Navajo County”
 
NOTICE OF PUBLIC HEARING AND AGENDA
Tuesday, January 9, 2024
NAVAJO COUNTY GOVERNMENTAL COMPLEX
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS' CHAMBERS
100 EAST CODE TALKERS DRIVE
HOLBROOK, AZ 86025
One or more supervisors may attend telephonically
Pursuant to A.R.S. §38-431.02(H), the public body will have physical access to the meeting place one (1) hour prior
to the start of the meeting. The Board may vote to meet in a closed executive session to discuss certain matters and
for legal advice on any item as indicated on the following agenda, pursuant to A.R.S. § 38-431.03(A)(3). Items on
the agenda may be considered out of order at the Chairperson's discretion. A copy of the agenda background
material provided to the Board, except items to be considered in the executive session, is available for public
inspection at the Navajo County website, www.navajocountyaz.gov 
WATCH THE MEETING LIVE AT: www.navajocountyaz.gov/660
All public comments will need to be made in person or in writing. Written comments will be received by the
Clerk of the Board's office twenty-four (24) hours prior to the Board meeting, at 
melissa.buckley@navajocountyaz.gov. If further accommodation is needed, please contact the Clerk of the
Board's office, at (928) 524-4053 twenty-four (24) hours prior to the Board Meeting, so accommodation can
be arranged.  
Persons with disabilities who need accommodation to attend or participate in the meeting may contact the Clerk of
the Board’s office at least forty-eight (48) hours prior to the meeting, so accommodation can be arranged.
“NOTICE TO PARENTS AND LEGAL GUARDIANS: Parents and legal guardians have the right to consent before Navajo
County makes a video or voice recording of a minor child, pursuant to A.R.S. § 1-602(A)(9). The Navajo County Board of
Supervisors' regular meetings are recorded and may be viewed on Navajo County’s website and social media pages. If you permit
your child to attend/participate in a televised Navajo County Board of Supervisors meeting, a recording will be made, and your
child’s picture may be posted on Navajo County’s social media pages. You may exercise your right not to consent by not
allowing your child to attend/participate in the meeting.”

NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS’ MINUTES
Tuesday, December 12, 2023
Present: Fern Benally, Supervisor District I; Alberto Peshlakai, Chairman; Jason
Whiting, Vice-Chairman; Daryl Seymore, Supervisor District IV 
Absent: Dawnafe Whitesinger, Supervisor District V 
Staff
Attendance:
Bryan Layton, County Manager; Brad Carlyon, County Attorney; Brandt
Clark, Deputy County Attorney (appearing via Zoom); Jason Moore,
Deputy County Attorney; Melissa Buckley, Clerk of the Board 
9:00 a.m
. Call To Order: Invocation and Pledge of Allegiance was led by Supervisor
Fern Benally.
The Board moved to Item 2, Consent Agenda. 
CALL TO THE PUBLIC:
Donna Gierst, White Mountain Lakes, provided the Board with a handout, and
addressed the Board regarding Agenda Item 4d, the White Mountain Lakes DWID.
 She talked about the steps that the community has taken to get try and get the water
district back up and running to allow them to try and purchase the water company
rather than a private company purchase it.  She indicated that she was told to get
community input and form an advisory committee.  She talked about the board that
was formed, indicating that it has been in effect for two months, and that an
introductory letter was sent to the Board of Supervisors.  She further talked about the
meetings that they have had with the Water Infrastructure Financial Authority.  She
indicated that the community got together to address advantages and disadvantages
for a DWID.  She indicated that there is an election in March.  She talked about the
proposed resolution and the County Improvement District guidebook addressing
dissolving a DWID.  She stated that the community is asking for an opportunity to look
into being able to get funding.    
Debi Bismarck, 8592 Fox Circle, White Mountain Lake, stated that she is on the
Community Association Board and talked about meetings that were held regarding the
DWID.  She stated that the community is not opposing the takeover, that they have a
lot of hardship in the area, and that they don’t need additional costs for another
company to come in.  
Eric Gierst, 1971 Daggs Circle, Show Low, stated that the DWID has been in place for
over thirty years.  He talked about the work that the community members have done
to have an opportunity to create and implement a DWID to manage their own water
system.  He indicated that they want to be recognized as a potential buyer for the
system.  He talked about the choices that are provided in the proposed resolution,
indicating that both of those choices will destroy the communities chance to try and
purchase.  He stated that they have a legal DWID in place now, and that they are
requesting that their Board be recognized either as an advisory committee or a
Board of Directors so they can take the steps necessary to move forward to try and
buy the water system.  He indicated that if the community doesn’t purchase it then
another company will, and they will suffer for it through their bill.  He stated that by
approving the resolution it takes away their opportunity.      
Rob Hossler, 1949 Norton Place, stated that they formed an advisory committee as
that he was selected as chairman.  He requested that they be given time to research
1 of 15 
DRAFT

<!-- page 9 -->
that he was selected as chairman.  He requested that they be given time to research
their options and determine if this will work for them.  He stated that there are a lot of
families in the community that are struggling and that if a private company comes in,
they will raise their rates.  He indicated that there is a lot of work that needs to be
done on the water district and that people will not be able to afford it.  He stated that
they would have the option of grants with reduced funding which would save the
community a lot of money to keep the water bills lower and more sustainable for
families.     He requested the opportunity to do wants right for the community.

BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:
 
a. Presentation to Snowflake High School Volleyball Team for winning the 2023
3A State Championship:
Vice-Chairman Whiting congratulated the Volleyball team on winning two
years in a row.  He stated that winning two years in a row is remarkable, great
coaching and a great program that starts at a young age.  He requested that
the coach and team captains speak about what it means to win state, what it
takes and advice they would give for anyone wanting to reach the goal of
state. 
The coach talked about what winning the state championship two times in a
row means, indicating that they work hard and put all of their focus into the
sport.  She indicated that they started the year with four injuries and that they
struggled at the beginning, but that the team showed determination to get
through that and came back to win.  She stated that she is proud of their hard
work and dedication.
The team captains talked about what winning state meant to them, their
experiences through the season and their appreciation for their coaches. 
Vice-Chairman Whiting talked about how this experience with winning state
is part of the journey in life.  He encouraged the team to carry what they have
done forward, to help lift and build those around them and be something great
and special.  He presented the team with medals in recognition of their
accomplishments.
 
  
b. Presentation to Snowflake High School Boys Soccer Team for winning the

b. Presentation to Snowflake High School Boys Soccer Team for winning the
2023 State Championship:
Vice-Chairman Whiting congratulated the Soccer Team on winning state and
talked about his experience while watching them play.  He stated that they
were fun to watch, especially as they came together as a team and a unit.  He
congratulated the coach, indicating that he did a wonderful job coming in new
and bringing the team together. 
The coach stated that their team moto was repetitions lead to consistency and
consistency wins championships.  He indicated that there was a lot of sacrifice
but that when the team realized that they could make it to state the intensity at
practice was up.  He stated that when you combine for the goal of being a
state champion it’s hard to take down someone who believes they can be a
champion. 
The team captains talked about what it takes to become a champion.
Vice-Chairman Whiting addressed the comments that the coach and
captions made regarding what it takes, indicating that they all stated a team
that was committed and unified.  He stated that when he watched them play,
they played as a unit.  He indicated that the championship was part of their
journey, that it is not everything in life, and that they should take what they
learned to help lift and build those around them.  He congratulated the team
on their championship and presented the team with medals in recognition of
their accomplishment.

9:50 a.m. The Board moved to Item 4a, Employee Recognition.
 
  
d. Consideration and adoption of Resolution Number 49-2023 revoking the
White Mountain Lakes Domestic Water Improvement District's Board of
Directors authority and further dissolving the White Mountain Lakes Domestic
Water Improvement District :
A presentation was shown.  Rochelle Lacapa talked about the water service
provisions in Navajo County, indicating that they are provided in multiple
ways, such as municipal water systems, private water companies via private
wells, and through Domestic Water Improvement Districts, which in turn is a
type of special taxing district and is governed by a separately elected board of
directors.  She indicated that Navajo County does not provide water services
and is not a utility provider anywhere in the county, that they rely on
  
                                                                    4 of 15 
DRAFT

<!-- page 12 -->
municipalities as well as other types of service providers to provide water. 
She stated that in July 2023 community members from White Mountain Lake
area started reaching out to county to reestablish DWID created in 1991 and
that they received feedback from other community members that it not be
reestablished, so it was unclear to staff if the community as a whole was in
favor.  She indicated that staff started researching options.  She provided
some background information regarding the 1991 DWID, indicating that it was
established in 1991, that the board was appointed by the Board of
Supervisors, that in 1993 the Board of Supervisors called for an elections
which was held October 12, 1993 but that is was never canvassed, a bond
was never passed and a tax was never collected, so the DWID existed on
paper but never fully functioned as a fully elected board and that it’s unclear
when the board became inactive.  She stated that according to statute, when
a special district becomes inactive or lacks a quorum for thirty days or more
the Board of Supervisors can revoke that authority and dissolve that special
district.  She indicated that they have worked with the Finance Department,
Elections, the Recorders Office, and County Attorney’s Office but were unable
to find any documents since the 1993 Board of Supervisors minutes.  She
talked about the current situation with the water company, which is owned by
Voyager, indicating that in March 2023 the sole proprietor passed away, that
the future of the company is unknown, and that the estate is going through
probate.  She indicated that this caused some uncertainty for the community
members and that some of the community members found the paperwork from
the 1991 DWID and reached out to the county about reestablishing the DWID
so they could take over operations, feeling that this would allow them more
control over water rates within the community rather than a private entity
coming in and possibly raising rates.  She stated that staff and would with the
legal department to determine what options the Board of Supervisors has. 
She talked about the options available to the Board, indicating that they can
revoke the authority and dissolve the special district, or they can revoke the
1991 DWID and call for elections.  She talked about the concerns,
advantages, and the disadvantages of both options, indicating that with either
option it doesn’t stop the private water company’s ability to still be sold.  She
stated that the community members are asking that the Board reestablish the
water district.  She further stated that staff recommends option one, that the
DWID be revoked and dissolved.
Vice-Chairman Whiting stated that he recognizes that the community is
trying to find potential solutions but indicated that they have to look at what’s
legal.  He inquired if the Board that the community put together has any
authority.
Jason Moore indicated that they don’t have any authority.  He indicated that
they don’t know who the last governing board members were since it was so
long ago, that they are operating on the assumption that all of the seats are
vacant at this point and addressed the law regarding fill vacancies.  He stated
that there is a third option, that the Board of Supervisors could revoke the
authority and the Board could run the district, but that Navajo County hasn’t
been in the business of being a public utility provider in the past, so they are
not recommending that option. 
A discussion was held regarding the different options. 
Vice-Chairman Whiting thanked the citizens for their statements.  He shared
his appreciation to the community members who worked to try to put together
                                                                    5 of 15 
DRAFT

<!-- page 13 -->
something and looked at a way to move forward.    
Vice-Chairman Whiting made a motion to adopt Resolution Number
49-2023 revoking the White Mountain Lakes Domestic Water Improvement
District's Board of Directors authority and further dissolving the White
Mountain Lakes Domestic Water Improvement District, motion seconded
by Supervisor Seymore.  Motion passed 4-0, with Supervisor Dawnafe
Whitesinger being absent.

REGULAR AGENDA:
 
1. Consideration of award of Contract No. B23-09-025 COVID-19
After-Action Report and Improvement Plan to Coll Consulting, LLC:
Catrina Jenkins introduced Kym O’Farrell, Navajo County Public Health
Emergency Preparedness Program Manager. 
Ms. O’Farrell stated that they are requesting of approval for Coll
Consulting to write the COVID After Action and Improvement Plan.  She
stated that there was a lot of work done internally by the Health
Department and in working with partners throughout the communities and
that they want to get it all documented.  She indicated that they were
selected from several submittals for the bid.    
Supervisor Whiting made a motion to approve award of Contract No.
B23-09-025 COVID-19 After-Action Report and Improvement Plan to Coll
Consulting, LLC, motion seconded by Supervisor Seymore.  Motion
passed 4-0, with Supervisor Dawnafe Whitesinger being absent. 
 
Supervisor Seymore made a motion to return to regular session, motion
seconded by Supervisor Benally.  Motion passed 4-0, with Supervisor
Dawnafe Whitesinger being absent.    
Vice-Chairman Whiting made a motion to enter into the Navajo County
Library District Board of Directors Session, motion seconded by
Supervisor Benally.  Motion passed 4-0, with Supervisor Dawnafe
Whitesinger being absent.
 
  
LIBRARY DISTRICT: Board of Directors Session: 
REGULAR AGENDA: 
 
1. Presentation of the Navajo County Library District 2023 year-end review:
 A presentation was shown.  Jennifer Volkert, Library District Director,
shared some highlights for the Library District, including that card holders
are up over 1,000 to make a total of 26,891 active users, that checkouts
are up almost 15,000 more than last year, that they added over 41,000
physical titles and 24,894 digital titles, and that they have added Hoopla
this year which gives them more than 1.5 million titles in over seven
formats.  She stated that it doesn’t only benefit their thirteen member
libraries but that they also have resource cards for the Navajo Nation
Window Rock branch.  She indicated that they have had over 38,000
computer sessions and over 6000 volunteer hours to help the district
operate.  She stated that the catalog searches are over 170,000 and
website visits were 48,000.  She further stated that the courier that they
  
                                                                    8 of 15 
DRAFT

<!-- page 16 -->
stated has transported 29,9119 items throughout the thirteen member
libraries and had over 1500 hours of driving. She indicated that they have
had the Cloud Library Movies and iBox books.  She stated that they were
able to get a grant that allowed them to hire two digital navigators. She
talked about the collaboration with the HR Department for the third quarter
wellness challenge and had one hundred new card holders in an
eight-week period.  She stated that they applied for and received eight
grants totaling more than $220,000.
Katie Pearson, Program Coordinator, talked about the training they
attended, including their first annual district wide training, a CPR Training
and professional development.  She stated that they had a Dyno
Encounter this summer where over 2000 library users and their guests
were able to participate in.  She indicated that countywide they have had
3102 events that more than 12,491 people have attended.  She stated that
they have created fifty-one program kits, with more in the works, and that
they created an E-device collection. 
Shannon Wilson, Library Courier, stated that she transports items through
the county libraries from Kayenta to Cibecue to rotate books throughout
the libraries. 
Rivke Huma, Digital Navigator, stated that she visits the thirteen member
libraries and answers digital questions that patrons may have.  She stated
that one of the main components of the grant that she works under is the
affordable connectivity program that helps get people in rural areas get
connected to the internet and provides digital literacy classes.  She
indicated that she is able to help teach people about the programs.            
 
Supervisor Seymore stated that he is grateful for all that the Library
District does and the support that they provide to the communities.
Chairman Peshlakai indicated that it’s great to see the staff at community
events throughout the County to provide outreach. 
 
Vice-Chairman Whiting made a motion to return to regular session,
motion seconded by Supervisor Benally.  Motion passed 4-0, with 
Supervisor Dawnafe Whitesinger being absent. 
 
PUBLIC WORKS:
 
PLANNING & DEVELOPMENT: 
 
1. Consideration of award of Contract #B23-08-002 Comprehensive Plan to
Logan Simpson Design, Inc:
Cody Cooper talked about the RFP process for this item, indicating that the
expectations that they put out were qualified consultants who would
conduct data collection and analysis, community engagement, visioning
and goal setting plan development, implementation strategy and plan
adoption, with the areas of emphasis for the submittals were related to
land use transportation, housing, which included an assessment of our
current housing as well as the future needs analysis, water resources,
energy usage, open space, as well as graphics integrated for the plan.  He
indicated that they received seven proposals, that a panel was put
together to evaluate and that after the evaluation and interviews the panel
  
                                                                    9 of 15 
DRAFT

<!-- page 17 -->
together to evaluate and that after the evaluation and interviews the panel
determined that Logan Simpson Design met the criteria.  He stated that
staff recommends approval.
Vice-Chairman Whiting made a motion to approve award of Contract
#B23-08-002 Comprehensive Plan to Logan Simpson Design, Inc, motion
seconded by Supervisor Seymore.  Motion passed 3-0, with Supervisor
Dawnafe Whitesinger and Supervisor Benally being absent. 
 
2. PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration of adoption of Resolution Number
50-2023, to allow for a Master Development Plan Amendment from an
approved, approximately 508-unit, single-family residential development
with commercial uses, to be amended to allow for recreational
vehicles/park models and manufactured homes for a total of 660 dwelling
units along with amenities, accessory uses, and commercial uses in the
Show Low Mountain Ranch Special Development within the White
Mountain Lake area: 
A presentation was shown.  Cody Cooper stated that this item was
previously tabled to allow for a community meeting and proposed changes
to be implemented.  He talked about the plan, location for the site, zoning
for the area, and the original site plan vs. the amended proposal that the
applicant is requesting be approved today.  He stated that they have
received four comments from the public against the project, with the major
focusing being traffic, water, sewer, lighting, prevention of code violations
on the properties themselves, worries about animal husbandry being
revoked, access to the site, emergency response times, drainage cell
reception and lack of internet in the area.  He indicated that based on the
staff analysis the item complies with the zoning ordinance and
comprehensive plan and does not present any concerns for the public
health, safety, and welfare.  He stated that staff recommends approval and
that the Planning & Zoning Commission had recommended approval of the
previous proposed plan.  He reviewed the recommended conditions for the
proposed project if approved.
A presentation was shown.  Greg Loper, applicant, addressed the Board
regarding the project and the revisions that have been made.       
Chairman Peshlakai opened the meeting for public comments.  There
being no comments, the public hearing was closed.
 
Supervisor Seymore inquired if the CC&R’s will be approved by the
Board and recorded.
Mr. Loper indicated that they would be recorded. 
Supervisor Seymore inquired what the difference between traffic impact
analysis and a category one analysis is.
Bill Bess stated that the difference is that an analysis has more detail.    
Supervisor Seymore requested that the conditions be changed to require
the greater of the two analysis.  He thanked Mr. Loper for the work that he
has done of this. 
 Vice-Chairman Whiting made a motion to adopt Resolution Number
50-2023, to allow for a Master Development Plan Amendment from an
  
                                                                    10 of 15 
DRAFT

<!-- page 18 -->
approved, approximately 508-unit, single-family residential development
with commercial uses, to be amended to allow for recreational
vehicles/park models and manufactured homes for a total of 660 dwelling
units along with amenities, accessory uses, and commercial uses in the
Show Low Mountain Ranch Special Development within the White
Mountain Lake area with the conditions stated by staff, motion seconded
by Supervisor Seymore.  Motion passed 4-0, with Supervisor Dawnafe
Whitesinger being absent. 
11:27 a.m. Chairman Alberto Peshlakai was excused from the meeting.  
Vice-Chairman Jason Whiting chaired the meeting.
 
3. PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration of adoption of Resolution Number
51-2023, approving/denying a Zone Change from R-3 to C-R to allow for a
54 space RV Park on a 4.65 acre parcel located north of
Pinetop/Lakeside, along Porter Mountain Road:
A presentation was shown.  Tyler Richards stated that this is a request for
a zone change.  He talked about the location of the property, the zoning in
the area, and provided a brief summary regarding the project.  He stated
that the proposal is in compliance with the comprehensive plan and has
been determined to be compatible with current and likely future uses.  He
indicated that there are minimal adverse effects on the public health,
safety, and general welfare in the area.  He stated that staff has received
a petition with twenty-five signatures against the zone change as well as
two comments against it.  He indicated that the Planning and Zoning
Commission unanimously voted to recommend approval at their
November 16, 2023, meeting.  He stated that staff recommends approval.
Bruce Ironside, Applicant, thanked the Board for consideration of this
project.  He stated that they feel this project fits in the community. 
Vice-Chairman Whiting opened the meeting for public comments. 
 
Brian Morris (appearing via Zoom), 3657 Pineway, Lakeside, stated that
he is appearing on behalf of the family that lives next to the property to
share their concerns.  He addressed concerns regarding diesel exhaust,
the noise from vehicles coming and going, and the construction activities
that will take place.  He talked about the need for a wall between the
properties as well as a landscape buffer.  He shared concerns regarding
the number of spaces for the proposed project and the light from the RV’s
shining into the neighbors property.    
 
11:41 a.m. Chairman Peshlakai (appearing via Zoom)
 
There being no additional comments, the public hearing was closed.
 
Supervisor Seymore requested that the questions asked by Mr. Morris
be answered.
Mr. Richards addressed concerns regarding the fencing, water, lighting,
and the club house. 
Mr. Ironside addressed the concerns regarding the fencing and the water. 
Supervisor Seymore talked about previous projects done by Mr. Ironside
and indicated that he is trustworthy. 
  
                                                                    11 of 15 
DRAFT

<!-- page 19 -->
Supervisor Seymore made a motion to adopt Resolution Number
51-2023, approving a Zone Change from R-3 to C-R to allow for a 54
space RV Park on a 4.65-acre parcel located north of Pinetop/Lakeside,
along Porter Mountain Road, motion seconded by Supervisor Benally. 
Motion passed 4-0, with Supervisor Dawnafe Whitesinger being absent. 
 
4. PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration of adoption of Resolution Number
52-2023, approving/denying a Special Use Permit to allow for construction
of a 54 space RV Park on a 4.65 acre parcel, located north of
Pinetop/Lakeside, along Porter Mountain Road:
A presentation was shown.  Tyler Richards stated that this item is for a
Special Use Permit for construction of a 54 space RV Park.  He talked
about the location of the proposed project and the site size.  He stated that
there will be solid fencing along the north and west boundary of the
property and that there will be additional three-to-six-foot landscaping
fence around the south and east portions of the property.  He stated that
they will make paved improvements to the entrance and that there is an
onsite well and sewer connection to Pinetop Lakeside Sanitary District.  He
talked about the staff analysis and stated that it does meet the zoning
ordinance and several goals of the Comprehensive Plan.  He advised that
the Planning and Zoning Commission recommended approval of the
Special Use Permit and that staff would also recommend approval.  He
reviewed the staff recommendations if the project is approved. 
Bruce Ironside, applicant, talked about the proposed project, indicating
that they feel this will be a good fit within the community.  He addressed
the concerns that were brought up regarding the fencing, water, sewer,
and trees.   
Vice-Chairman Whiting opened the meeting for public comments. 
 
Brian Morris (appearing via Zoom), 3657 Pineway, Lakeside, addressed
the proposed project.  He stated that he did look at some of the applicants
other projects and that it appears they do good work.  He requested that
some of the concerns that were previously addressed be documented so
the residents are aware of what to expect. 
 
There being no additional comments, the public hearing was closed.
Supervisor Seymore made a motion to adopt Resolution Number
52-2023, approving a Special Use Permit to allow for construction of a 54
space RV Park on a 4.65 acre parcel, located north of Pinetop/Lakeside,
along Porter Mountain Road, with review in 3 years and same conditions
as the zone change, motion seconded by Supervisor Benally.  Motion
passed 4-0, with Supervisor Dawnafe Whitesinger being absent. 
 
  
5. PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and possible adoption of Resolution   
                                                                    12 of 15 
DRAFT

<!-- page 20 -->
5. PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and possible adoption of Resolution
Number 53-2023 approving/denying a request by Pinnacle Consulting,
Inc. for a Special Use Permit to allow for the construction of a 100'
monopole wireless communication tower and associated facilities in the
Heber area:
A presentation was shown.  Sondra Starke stated that this item is for a call
tower.  She addressed the location of the proposed project, indicating that
the current use is commercial, and that zoning is industrial.  She stated
that there will be a six-foot chain-link fence around the project area and
that there is a dedicated access and utility easement.  She advised that
the Planning and Zoning Commission did unanimously recommend
approval with no additional conditions.  She reviewed the site plan and the
minimum setbacks, indicating that it is in compliance.  She indicated that
this has been properly noticed and advertised but that it was not posted
due to inaccessibility.  She further indicated that there were note cards
mailed out.  She stated that this proposal is in compliance with the zoning
ordinance, that it meets several goals of the comprehensive plan and use
has been determined as compatible with current and likely future uses. 
She indicated that they haven’t received any public comments.  She
stated that staff recommends approval with the conditions outlined in the
staff report. 
 
John Katsiris, Pinnacle Consulting, (appearing via Zoom), talked about the
location for the tower, indicating that it is an industrial zone and that there
is a commercial company on the property.   He indicated that the setback
meets the requirements.  He stated that the area is in need of upgraded
service and that there has been no community opposition. 
Vice-Chairman Whiting opened the meeting for public comments. There
being no comments, the public hearing was closed.
 
Supervisor Seymore requested if the tower could be monopine instead of
monopole due to the pine trees and the aesthetics of the area. 
Mr. Katsiris addressed the reason that the monopine was not used. 
A discussion was held regarding the type of pole that is being used. 
Supervisor Seymore made a motion to adopt Resolution Number
53-2023 approving a request by Pinnacle Consulting, Inc. for a Special
Use Permit to allow for the construction of a 100' monopole wireless
communication tower and associated facilities in the Heber area, motion
seconded by Supervisor Benally.  Motion passed 4-0, with Supervisor
Dawnafe Whitesinger being absent. 
 
  
6. PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and adoption of Resolution Number
54-2023 approving/denying a Special Use Permit to allow for the
construction and operation of a 112.5 MWdc solar energy generation
facility, 75 MW Battery Energy Storage System, and associated facilities
on approximately 625 acres located in the Snowflake area :
A presentation was shown.  Cody Cooper stated that this is for a solar
generation facility as well as battery storage.  He addressed the location of
the project, indicating that it would be for one hundred twelve and a half
  
                                                                    13 of 15 
DRAFT

<!-- page 21 -->
megawatts of DC storage and for seventy-five megawatts of battery
storage.  He talked about the access points to the location, construction
management and the operations for the project.  He reviewed the site plan
and indicated that the applicant has completed reviews for cultural
resources, plant and wildlife populations, as well as wetland and stream
features and that the site has been found to have little to no impact on
those resources based on the review.  He stated that the applicant has
also worked wit5h the State Fire Marshal’s Office as well as the local fire
jurisdiction to ensure that fire protection will be provided and the project
and that is goes through the proper fire code.  He indicated that the
Planning and Zoning Commission unanimously recommended approval of
the project, that staff has not received any comments and that it is
compliant with the relevant goals and policies of the comprehensive plan
and will not have any effect on the public health, safety and welfare based
on the conditions provided.  He stated that staff is recommending approval
subject to the conditions listed in the staff report.
A presentation was shown.  Don Bugles, applicant, stated that the
company is based out of Denver and that they develop renewable energy
projects all over the country.  He talked about the proposed project,
indicating that it will not alter the footprint or any aspect of the design to
accommodate the project.  He addressed the location of the proposed
project, stating that there are no residential neighbors in the area.  He
talked about the economic impact for the project, indicating that it will
generate tax revenue and create jobs during the construction as well as
several permanent maintenance jobs.  He advised that the power will be
generated and absorbed locally and that it will be owned by a group of
private investors so there will be no rate increases from APS. 
Vice-Chairman Whiting opened the meeting for public comments. There
being no comments, the public hearing was closed.
 
Supervisor Seymore requested that staff, on decommissioning, follow the
guidelines as much as possible and that the bond requirements be
reevaluated every three to five years. 
Supervisor Seymore made a motion to adopt Resolution Number
54-2023 approving a Special Use Permit to allow for the construction and
operation of a 112.5 MWdc solar energy generation facility, 75 MW Battery
Energy Storage System, and associated facilities on approximately 625
acres located in the Snowflake area, motion seconded
by Chairman Peshlakai.  Motion passed 4-0, with Supervisor Dawnafe
Whitesinger being absent.

and Board members :

County Christmas Party.
Supervisor Seymore reported on being able to finish up a year project for the jail
service with public health, indicating that it went really well.  He talked about the
need to change the way we look at people who have addictions and shared a story
regarding the reason for changing our perspective.  He wished everyone a Merry

approval of the item for the grader operators.  She wished everyone a Happy
Holidays. 
Vice-Chairman Whiting wished everyone a Merry Christmas and Happy Holidays.

Page -1- 
 
 
 
 
  
 
 
 CONTRACT FOR PROFESSIONAL SERVICES  
 
 
AGREEMENT made as of December 8, 2023, between Navajo County ("County") 
and Kimley-Horn and Associates, Inc. (“Consultant”). 
 
WHEREAS, County requires certain profession al services for On-Call Traffic 
Engineering Services Project (“Project”); and 
 
WHEREAS, Consultant represents that it has the necessary expertise to provide such 
services in accordance with the terms of this Agreement. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the parties agree as follows: 
 
1. Services.  Consultant shall perform the work described in the Scope of Work dated 
November 29, 2023, and those additional items set forth in the Consultant’s schedule 
and fee proposal dated November 29, 2023, in a competent and professional manner 
to the satisfaction of County.  The Scope of Work and the Consultant’s schedule and 
fee proposal are attached he reto and by this reference incorporated herein.  If any 
incorporated term is inconsistent with the Agreement, this Agreement shall control.   
 
2. Consultant’s Expertise.  Consultant warrants that it has the ability, authority, 
capacity and professional expertise to perfo rm this Agreement.  Consultant shall 
provide an adequate staff of experienced  personnel, capable of and devoted to the 
successful accomplishment of the work to be performed hereunder.  Consultant shall 
assign specific individuals to key positio ns.  Once assigned to work under this 
Agreement, key personnel shall not be removed or replaced without County’s prior 
written approval.  
 
3. Independent Contractor.  Consultant acknowledges that  it is an independent 
contractor.  Neither party is or shall purport to be an agent, employee, partner, joint 
venturer or associate of the other.  An employee or agen t of one party shall not be 
deemed an employer or agent  of the other party for any purpose.  Taxes or Social 
Security contributions will not be with held from any payment by County, and 
Consultant shall be solely responsible for such matters.

<!-- page 432 -->
Page -2- 
4. Subcontracts.  Consultant shall not enter into any subcontract with respect to any of 
the work to be performed hereunder without County’s prior written approval.  All 
subcontracts shall comply with applicable federal and state laws and regulations and 
shall impose on the subcontractor substantially the same obligations as are imposed 
on Consultant by this Agreement with respect to those matters covered by Sections 8, 
9, 10, 12, 15 and 18.  Consultant is  responsible for full performance of this 
Agreement regardless of whether subcontractors are used. 
 
5. Time for Completion.  Consultant’s services will be provided in a manner consistent 
with the degree of care and skill ordi narily exercised by me mbers of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances.  Consultant’s services 
shall be performed as expeditiously as is consistent with professional skill and care 
and the orderly progress of the Project, and in accord ance with an agreed upon 
schedule for the performance of the Services hereunder.  Time limits established by a 
schedule approved by County shall not, except for reasonable cause, be exceeded by 
County or Consultant.  The Schedule shall be adjusted, if  necessary, as the Project 
proceeds.  The date for final performance shall be extended by the number of days 
that performance is delayed by governmental approval or review procedures or other 
causes beyond Consultant’s reasonable control, as jointly confirmed in writing by the 
parties’ representatives. 
 
6. Payment.  County shall pay to Consultant a not-to-exceed sum of forty thousand 
dollars ($40,000.00) as payment in full for all services rendered by Consultant 
pursuant to this Agre ement.  Payments shall be made  within 30 days of County’s 
receipt of Consultant’s monthly invoices .  Each invoice shal l detail the work 
performed during the billing period.  Invoice amounts in percent of Project cost shall 
not exceed the percentage of completion of the Project as approved by County.   
 
7. Defects in Work.  County may reject any work product that fails to meet customary 
professional standards or Project specifi cations.  Consultant agrees to promptly 
remedy all such deficiencies.  The parties shall make a good faith effort to resolve any 
controversy or claim through informal nego tiation as set forth in Section 12.  No 
compensation shall be paid for any reject ed work until such issues have been 
resolved. 
 
8. Insurance Requirements.   
 
A. Consultant shall maintain in effect , at all times during the term of this 
Agreement, insurance adequate to protect County and its agents, 
representatives, officers, officials and employees against such losses as set 
forth below.  Consultant shall provide County with a current Certificate of 
Insurance or a certified copy of the insurance policy naming County as an 
additional insured (except for Errors an d Omissions / Professional Liability 
coverage). 
 
 
B. The following types an d amounts of insurance are required as minimums:

<!-- page 433 -->
Page -3- 
  
Worker's Compensation    Statutory 
Professional Liability    $1,000,000 each occurrence 
           and annual aggregate 
Consultant’s Protective Bodily Injury  $1,000,000 each occurrence  
           and annual aggregate 
Consultant’s Protective Personal Property $1,000,000 each occurrence  
           and annual aggregate 
Automobile Bodily Injury and Property Damage $1,000,000 each 
occurrence 
       and annual aggregate 
Valuable Papers     $100,000 
 
Standard minimum deductibles are allowed.  Any deductibles are the 
responsibility of Consultant.  Consultant shall immediately inform County of 
any cancellation of insurance or any decrease in the amount of coverage at 
least 30 days before such action takes place.  A violation of this provision may 
be treated as a material breach by Co unty.  Consultant shall notify County 
upon any termination of its regular prof essional liability coverage and shall 
obtain tail coverage for a minimum of five years from the termination date. 
 
C. Consultant acknowledges that the amounts of insurance coverage stated above 
shall not limit its liability under this Agreement. 
 
9. Indemnity.  To the fullest extent allowable  by law, Consultant shall defend, 
indemnify and hold harmless County and its  representatives, officers, officials and 
employees (the “Indemnitees”) from and against any and all claims, damages, losses 
and expenses (including but not limited to attorney fees, court costs, and the costs of 
appellate proceedings) relating to, arising out of or resulting from Consultant’s 
negligent acts, errors, mistakes or omissions in the performance of this Agreement. 
Consultant’s duty to defend, hold harmless and indemnify the Indemnitees shall arise 
in connection with any claim, damage, loss or expense that is attributable to bodily 
injury, sickness, disease, death or injury to, impairment or destruction of property 
including loss of use resulting therefrom, caused in whole or in part by any negligent 
act, error, mistake or omission in the performance of this Agreement (including those 
by any person for whose negligent acts, errors, mistakes or omissions Consultant may 
be liable) to the extent of Consultant’s negligence or fault.  The amount and type of 
insurance coverage requirements set fort h above shall not lim it the scope of the 
indemnity in this paragraph. 
 
10. Records.  Consultant shall retain, and shall require each subcontractor to retain, all 
books, accounts, reports, files and other reco rds relating to this Agreement for a 
period of five years after completion of the work.  All such documents shall be 
subject to inspection and audit by Coun ty upon reasonable notice during normal 
business hours.  A legible co py of any or all such documents shall be produced by 
Consultant at the request and expense of County.

<!-- page 434 -->
Page -4- 
11. Non-Assignment.  Consultant shall not assign any right or interest in this Agreement 
without County’s prior written approval, nor  shall Consultant delegate any duty 
hereunder without County’s prior written approval.   
 
12. Negotiation of Disputes.  The parties shall make a good faith effort to resolve any 
claim or controversy or claim through informal negotiation.  Notice of any claim or 
controversy shall be provided in writi ng, with supporting doc umentation, to the 
recipient designated in Section 21.  The recipient shall have seven calendar days to 
prepare and deliver a written response.  If the parties fail to resolve the disputes 
within a reasonable period of not less than ten days, either party may pursue available 
legal remedies. 
 
13. Suspension and Termination by County: 
 
A. Suspension.  County may, without cause, order Consultant in writing to suspend, 
delay or interrupt its performance in whole or part.  An adjustment shall be made 
to the completion date and for any increase in the cost of performance resulting 
from the suspension, delay or interruption.  No adjustment shall be made to the 
extent that performance is or would have been suspended, delayed or interrupted 
by another cause for which Consultant is responsible. 
 
B. Termination for Cause.   County may terminate th is Agreement for cause if 
Consultant refuses or fails to supply enough properly skilled workers to perform 
this Agreement; fails to make required payments to subcontractors; disregards 
laws, ordinances, rules, regulations or  orders of a public  authority having 
jurisdiction; or otherwise commits a material breach of  this Agreement.  When 
any of the foregoing causes exist, County shall give Consultant written notice and 
a ten-day opportunity to cure the default.  If the default remains uncured, County 
may (without prejudice to any other rights or remedies it may have) terminate this 
Agreement and finish the work or cause it to be finished.  If the unpaid balance of 
the Agreement exceeds the cost of fini shing the work (including any expenses 
incurred by County as a result of Cons ultant’s default), C onsultant shall be 
entitled to payment for its performance up to the amount of such excess.  If the 
cost of finishing the work exceeds the u npaid balance, Consultant shall pay the 
difference to County. 
 
C. Termination for Convenience.   County, by written n otice to Consultant, may 
terminate this Agreement in whole or part when in the sole discretion of County it 
is in County’s best interests to do so.  Consultant shall be pa id for all material, 
equipment and services provided, as well as reasonable termination expenses and 
a reasonable allowance for profit and overhead, provided that such payments, 
exclusive of termination expenses, shal l not exceed the total amount payable 
pursuant to Section 6 less any paymen ts previously made to Consultant.  
Consultant shall not be entitled to profit and overhead on material, equipment and 
services which were not provided. 
 
D. Consultant’s Responsibilities.  Upon receipt of a termination notice, Consultant

<!-- page 435 -->
Page -5- 
shall (a) promptly discontinue all servic es (unless the notice directs otherwise), 
and (b) deliver or otherwise make available to County copies of all data, design 
calculations, drawings, specifications, reports, estimates, summaries and other 
information and materials developed or accumulated by Consultant in performing 
this Agreement. 
 
14. Termination by Consultant.  Consultant may terminate this Agreement upon written 
notice to County if performance is made impossible for a period of 30 consecutive 
calendar days for any of the following reasons through no act or fault of Consultant or 
its agents, employees or subcontractors: issuance of an order by a court or other 
public authority having jurisdiction; an act  of government, such as a declaration of 
national emergency; or a natural disaster or other Act of God.  Consultant may also 
terminate this Agreement upon written notice  and a ten-day opportunity to cure if 
County fails to make any payment within the time set forth in Section 6.  Consultant 
may also terminate this Ag reement upon written notice if  suspensions, delays or 
interruptions by County equal in the aggregate more than 100% of the total number of 
days scheduled for completion. 
 
15. Governing Law.   This Agreement shall  be governed by the law of the State  of 
Arizona.  Any suit arising out of this Agreement shall be brought in the state courts of 
Arizona (with venue in Navajo County) or the federal District of Arizona, but only 
after informal negotiation pursuant to Section 12. 
 
16. Incorporation of Applicable Laws.  Every provision of law required by statute or 
regulation to be in this Agreement will be read and enforced as though included 
herein.  Each party shall promptly notify the other upon discovery that any such 
provision has been omitted. 
 
17. Non-Collusion.  The provisions of A.R.S. § 38-511 are incorporated herein by this 
reference. 
 
18. One-Year Limitation on Actions.  No action shall be maintained by Consultant on 
any claim based upon or aris ing out of this Agreement unless such action is 
commenced within one year after County’s final payment hereunder. 
 
19. Term.  This Contract for Professional Service is valid for one (1) year from the date 
the agreement was made between the two parties. Upon mutual agreement between 
the parties, this contract may be renewed up to four (4) additional one-year terms. 
 
20. Entire Agreement.  This Agreement and those 
documents incorporated by reference 
represent the entire understanding between  the parties.  No amendment shall be 
effective unless executed by both parties with the same formality as this Agreement.  
Provided, however, that the County’s project  manager is authorized to modify the 
Scope of Work, in writing, with the concurrence of Consultant so long as the project 
is not changed substantially or significant additional compensation is not required. 
 
21. Severability.  The provisions of this Agreement are severable.  Any provision held to

<!-- page 436 -->
Page -6- 
be invalid or unenforceable shall not affect the validity or enforceability of any other 
provision. 
 
22. Notices.  All notices, invoices and payments shall be in writing and may be given by 
personal delivery or certified mail.  The designated recipients are as follows:

RE: Traffic Engineering On-Call Professional Services
Dear Mr. Flake:
Kimley-Horn is grateful for the opportunity to provide Navajo County with traffic
engineering on-call professional services on an as-needed basis on various projects
at the request of the Navajo County Public Works Department.
Scope of Services
Anticipated traffic engineering on-call services include, but are not limited to: traffic
analysis and design, transportation planning, traffic operations and safety, review of
submittals by others, preparation of grant applications, program development and
management, and public/stakeholder engagement.
Compensation
Kimley-Horn will provide these services on a time and materials basis for a not-to-
exceed fee of $40,000 per on-call task order. Invoices will be provided no more
frequently than monthly and will use the loaded hourly rates shown on the subsequent
page for effort expended during the invoice period. Kimley-Horn will provide an
estimated fee range to Navajo County before beginning any on-call activities.
Please feel free to contact me at (480) 207-2662 (michael.grandy@kimley-horn.com)
should you have any questions or require any additional information.
Sincerely,
Michael Grandy, P.E.
Vice President

Board of Supervisors Regular 3. j.        
Meeting Date: 01/09/2024  
Title: High School Internship Program Agreement
Submitted For: Mischa Larisch, Deputy Director of Planning & Development Services 
Submitted By: Mischa Larisch, Deputy Director of Planning & Development Services
Department: Public Works Division: Planning & Development
Motion before the Board:
High School Internship Program Agreement with Northern Arizona Academy
Background:
Northern Arizona Academy (NAA) has a grant that pays employers $2000 for 120 hours of
internship for high school students.  NAA is looking for several employers who are willing to
take on a high school student to help them gain knowledge in various areas of the workforce. 
The internships help high school students learn about various careers within our community. 
This allows students to experience a career that they might have an interest in before deciding on
that particular career path.

Title: Purchase of 2024 Ford Transit 350 ADA Van
Submitted For: David Clouse, Sheriff 
Submitted By: Kimberley Willis, Sheriff's Office Finance Manager
Department: Sheriff 
Motion before the Board:
Purchase of a new 2024 Ford Transit 350 ADA cargo van
Background:
The Navajo County Sheriff's Office was approved for 50% or up to $75,000 towards the
purchase of an ADA Compliant Transport Van. With a significant amount of reimbursement
coming back to the Jail District from this same grant ($75,336 per year to recoup jail medical
expenses relative to border crime as well as $412,650 annually to off-set border related booking
and housing costs), the matching 50% will not impact the FY24 Jail District budget. This
purchase exceeds the $100,000.00 procurement cap and accordingly, the Office asks for
approval to proceed with the purchase in the amount of $121,603.40 through San Tan Ford who
is a state contract vendor.

1.10.1. Volts - 12

1.18. PARTITIONS/SEATS/LINING/FLOOR:  
1.18.1. Construction Materials: 
1.18.1.1. Partitions - Square tubing 1" .083 gauge expanded metal ½” 13 flat. 
1.18.1.2. Doors - Square tubing 1" .083 gauge expanded metal ½” 13 flat. 
1.18.1.3. Door Latches - Flat Bar Stock, 3/8" x 1".

<!-- page 479 -->
1.18.1.4. Interior Lining - Aluminum sheeting minimum thickness .050”  
1.18.1.5. Bench Seats - Steel sheeting, 14-gauge, square tubing 2" .083 gauge. 
1.18.1.6. Hinges - Heavy duty steel. 
1.18.1.7. Locks - Double cylinder dead bolts shall be installed with special one- way type 
screws. 
1.18.1.8. Floor Covering Rear Area - Spray applied polyurethane, minimum 1/16" thick, color 
black. 
1.18.1.9. Rear Step - Square tubing 2", flat steel 1/8", angle iron 2" x 2", steel diamond plate. 
1.18.1.10. Side/Rear Door Padlock Brackets - Angle iron 2" x 2". 
 
1.18.2. Partitions: 
1.18.2.1. Front Partition – Starts from the floor to the interior roof and from the right interior 
to the left interior walls. The bottom section shall be a 14-gauge metal, with vertical 
supports every 14 1/2". The top half shall be expanded metal Etched  3/16 poly 
carbonate installed over the expanded metal, on the front side of this partition. 
1.18.2.2. Center Partition – Installed from the floor to the interior roof and from the right 
interior to the left interior walls. The bottom section shall be 28" high with vertical 
supports every 14 1/2", the top section shall have two escape doors 22" x 24" shall 
be installed in the top center section, hinged at the center, dead bolt type locks, 
door to open either direction. Partition shall be lined with .050 aluminum installed 
with 3/16" steel button head pop rivets 
1.18.2.3. Rear Area Center Divider Partition - Installed from the floor to the interior roof  and 
connected to the front center and rear partitions, with vertical supports every 14".  
Partition shall be lined with .050 aluminum installed with 3/16" steel button head pop 
rivets. 
1.18.2.4. Rear Door/Partition - Installed from the floor to the interior roof and from the right 
interior to the left interior walls. Shall have two (2) 25" wide, 42" high doors, with 
centered vertical and horizontal supports, hinged at the center, three- point latch 
mechanisms with 24" spread, padlock latch.  
1.18.2.5. Construction - All partitions shall be designed and constructed such that all 
maintenance items such as the AC, heater, lights and etc. are easily accessible. 
Panels, covers and etc. that are easy to remove shall be provided where access is 
necessary. Partition shall be designed such that they are easily bolted together to 
form the individual prisoner cages. Filler panels for the front and center partitions 
shall be designed and constructed to fit the contour of van walls, riveted to each end 
of the partitions in such a way that they can be easily removed. Frame work for 
partition and doors shall have full penetrating welds at every joint. Expanded metal 
to have full penetrating welds at every contracting surface, approximately every 1/2” 
to 1” depending on metal positioning. Bottom section of front partition shall be 
covered with 16-gauge sheet metal, tack welded every 3”.  Heavy-duty steel hinges 
to be welded on. Door latch brackets shall be constructed of the same material as 
latch mechanisms.  Bolts with self -locking nuts shall be used at all partition to 
partition connecting points, 3/16” steel pop rivets to be used to attach partition to van 
body. A small amount of silicon sealer shall be applied to each contacting point at 
the time of assembly eliminating all rattles and squeaks.  Left side of front partition 
where it contacts side wall shall be sealed with silicon sealer upward 12”, making a 
water proof corner. 
1.18.3. Bench Seats: 
1.18.3.1. Front Compartment Benches - 14" wide, 44" long, fold down design, one 
installed across the van parallel to the front partition and one installed 
parallel with the center partition. The seats shall not interfere with the 
wheelchair restraint system, when in the f old down position, when not in 
use. 
1.18.3.2. Rear Compartment Benches -  Two (2), overall dimension 14" wide, 64" 
long, one (1) in each compartment, positioned parallel with outside wall

<!-- page 480 -->
directly rearward of center partition. 
1.18.3.3. Construction - Full penetrating weld at all joints, non-skid seating surface, 
anchored to the floor.  
1.18.3.4. Interior Lining:  The complete rear interior roof and side walls shall be lined 
with minimum .050” aluminum sheet metal, installed with 3/16" steel button 
head pop rivets, all joints shall be sealed with silicon and completely water 
proof so entire unit can be hosed out if necessary. 
1.18.3.5. Floor Coating:  Black polyurethane coating, minimum 1/16" thickness shall 
be applied to the entire rear floor area extending a minimum over the bench 
tops and front partition. Coating shall be applied such that all components 
installed on the floor shall be completely sealed so that water or other 
liquids will not be trapped under the bench seats or other components.  
1.18.3.6. Padlock Brackets, Side and Rear Door:  Overall dimensions, 2" x 2" x 1/8" 
x 2" installed on each door providing a means for padlocking side and rear 
doors. Brackets to be attached to doors with 3/16" steel pop rivets using a 
small amount of silicon sealer on the contacting surfaces at the time of 
assembly. See drawings.

REAR STEP:

1.24. KEYS: 
Each unit shall have four (4) sets of keys with rings, tags and be properly identified. 
 
1.25. GENERAL: 
Electrical wiring installed by the dealer or sub-contractor shall follow standard vehicle 
manufacturer’s procedures, including color coding, conduit, nylon ties, grommets (sealed 
type) for holes in metal, any opening made in the cab shall be sealed to prevent fumes from

terminals such as Ford’s Power Distribution Center.  
 
WARRANTY OFFERED:

PROJECT SCHEDULING 
Production time to build quoted unit is 60- 120 days from receipt of chassis. The start date for the purpose

3) Notification of date Vehicle Chassis is scheduled for delivery to QVSV’s facility in Tem pe, AZ. 
4) All initial design details completed, and questions answered between customer and QVSV.   
5) Delays by customer might impact delivery schedule. 
Quality Vans has been in business since 1974.  We are highly experienced in the design and manufacture 
of specialty vehicles for all types of uses including law enforcement, federal agencies, medical facilities,

<!-- page 500 -->
HOCRN
•Who is HOCRN?
•When was HOCRN established?
•What has HOCRN achieved?
•Why does HOCRN work?
•Where is HOCRN’s future?

<!-- page 501 -->
Who is HOCRN?
• HOCRN, Heber-Overgaard Community Resource 
Network
• A non-profit 501c3 serving the Heber-Overgaard 
residents and visitors
• Operated 100% by VOLUNTEERS
• HOCRN Thrift Store
• HOCRN Community Van

<!-- page 502 -->
When was HOCRN established?
• HOCRN, was established 02/22/22
• HOCRN idea of Fire Chief William D. McCluskey and 
local Realtor, June Call
• HOCRN originally started to handle all Emergency 
and Non-Emergency requests from our local Fire 
Department and Sheriff Department
•Mission Statement:  “To provide 
coordination of community 
resources for Heber-Overgaard 
residents and visitors.”

<!-- page 503 -->
What has HOCRN achieved?
• Christmas Adoptions (through the local school district) 
• Senior Angel Tree 
• HOCRN Thrift Store
• HOCRN Community Van
• HOCRN Scholarships to MHS ($8,000 annually)
• Community resource and responses
• Heber-Overgaard Residents
• Visitors

<!-- page 504 -->
HOCRN Thrift Store
• July 29, 2022, HOCRN took over existing Thrift Store in H-O
• At the time HOCRN had a whopping $37 in our Bank Account
• HOCRN Thrift Store was literally the “BEGINNING of the KEY 
to OUR SUCCESS”
• HOCRN Thrift Store is operated 100% by VOLUNTEERS
• Volunteers sort and price donated items, cashier and clerk, 
stage and decorate for the holidays, volunteer team picks up 
furniture and estate items
• Minimal items make it to trash dumpster
• Resources are utilized, i.e., Sunshine Acres, Fishers of Men, 
LATCH, Tall Pines Animal Rescue, other Thrift Stores

<!-- page 505 -->
HOCRN 
Thrift 
Store

<!-- page 506 -->

<!-- page 507 -->

Denise,

<!-- page 508 -->
GrowTogether,                      
HOCRN Community Garden
• HOCRN’s very first Project
• Local gardening expert, plus Master Gardener’s from 
Tempe and Fountain Hills living in H-O offered support 
and guidance
• HOCRN has 20 year FUA with H-O Schools for garden 
location
• Space provides room for growth and additional projects
• Garden Volunteers built the garden from the ground up
• Grow Together, HOCRN Community Garden completed on 
time as planting began May 1, 2023

and Sheri at

<!-- page 512 -->
HOCRN’s first community project,               
Grow Together, HOCRN Community Garden
14

<!-- page 513 -->
GrowTogether,                            
HOCRN Community Garden
• Garden consists of 34 beds, 4’ X 12’, rent annually for $120
• Two larger horseshoe shaped beds, sponsored and available 
for Mogollon Schools and Rim Country Senior Center
• Two herb beds provide herbs for gardeners and FOG
• ALL beds made from redwood lumber, expensive but should 
provide 10-12 years of use 
• Eight foot fence surrounds garden to keep elk out
• Timers are provided to regulate watering needs

can share at our meetings, all of these encourage people to 
give, either volunteering, donating to Thrift, or financially

Board of Supervisors Regular 5. a.        
Meeting Date: 01/09/2024  
Title: Professional Real Estate Services No. B23-09-027
Submitted By: Cheryl Hollins, Account Specialist
Department: Administrative Services
Presented By: Grant Evans
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of award of contract B23-09-027 for on-call Professional Real Estate Services to
Jeffery Millet, SEI Real Estate Professionals.
Background:
This contract is to retain a consultant to provide on-call Professional Real Estate Services within
Navajo County. On-call Professional Real Estate Services would be used when the County is in
need of assistance to purchase, sell, or lease property, such as for future growth, right-of-way
acquisition, property leases with partner agencies, etc.  Due to the range of different types of
properties and the variety of different scopes of work that the County may need when looking to
buy, sell, or lease property, the County will negotiate a scope of work and a market standard fee
(if applicable) for each task assignment/transaction individually.  Sealed proposals were
received by the Board of Supervisors at the Navajo County Governmental Center, Holbrook,
Arizona until 4:00 p.m., M.S.T, November 8, 2023.  No proposals were accepted after 4:00
p.m.  The proposals were publicly opened and read aloud at 4:15 p.m.  One proposal was
received and evaluated per the criteria set forth in the request for qualifications.
Recommendation: 
The Evaluation Committee is recommending award of Contract B23-09-027 Professional Real
Estate Services to Jeffery Millet, SEI Real Estate Professionals.

 CONTRACT FOR PROFESSIONAL SERVICES  
 
AGREEMENT made as of January 9, 2024 between Navajo County ("County") and 
Jeffery Millett, SEI Real Estate Professionals. (“Consultant”). 
 
WHEREAS, County requires certain profession al services for Navajo County 
Professional Real Estate Services Contract No. B23-09-027 (“Project”); and 
 
WHEREAS, Consultant represents that it has the necessary expertise to provide such 
services in accordance with the terms of this Agreement. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the parties agree as follows: 
 
1. Services & Compensation.   Consultant shall perform the work described in the 
Request for Qualifications dated November 6, 2023 in a competent and professional 
manner to the satisfaction of County.  The compensation and level of service required 
will be negotiated between County and Consultant prior to each transaction. 
 
2. Contract Documents.
 The Contract Documents comprise this Contract, Request for 
Qualifications No. B23-09-027, Consultants Proposal dated November 6, 2023 and 
addendums thereto, if any. If any incorporated term is inconsistent with the Contract, 
this Contract shall control over all others; the Request for Qualifications shall control 
over the Consultants proposal.. 
 
3. Consultant’s Expertise.  Consultant warrants that it has the ability, authority, 
capacity and professional expertise to perfo rm this Agreement.  Consultant shall 
provide an adequate staff of experienced  personnel, capable of and devoted to the 
successful accomplishment of the work to be performed hereunder.  Consultant shall 
assign specific individuals to key positio ns.  Once assigned to work under this 
Agreement, key personnel shall not be removed or replaced without County’s prior 
written approval.  
 
4. Independent Contractor.  Consultant acknowledges that  it is an independent 
contractor.  Neither party is or shall purport to be an agent, employee, partner, joint 
venturer or associate of the other.  An employee or agen t of one party shall not be 
deemed an employer or agent  of the other party for any purpose.  Taxes or Social 
Security contributions will not be w ithheld from any paym ent by County, and 
Consultant shall be solely responsible for such matters.

<!-- page 538 -->
Page -2- 
5. Subcontracts.  Consultant shall not enter into any subcontract with respect to any of 
the work to be performed hereunder without County’s prior written approval.  All 
subcontracts shall comply with applicable federal and state laws and regulations and 
shall impose on the subcontractor substantially the same obligations as are imposed 
on Consultant by this Agreement with respect to those matters covered by Sections 9, 
10, 11, 13, 16, 19, 22 and 23.  Consultant is responsible for full performance of this 
Agreement regardless of whether subcontractors are used. 
 
6. Time for Completion.  Consultant’s services will be provided in a manner consistent 
with the degree of care and skill ordi narily exercised by me mbers of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances.  Consultant’s services 
shall be performed as expeditiously as is consistent with professional skill and care 
and the orderly progress of the Project, and in accord ance with an agreed upon 
schedule for the performance of the Services hereunder.  Time limits established by a 
schedule approved by County shall not, except for reasonable cause, be exceeded by 
County or Consultant.  The Schedule shall be adjusted, if  necessary, as the Project 
proceeds.  The date for final performance shall be extended by the number of days 
that performance is delayed by governmental approval or review procedures or other 
causes beyond Consultant’s reasonable control, as jointly confirmed in writing by the 
parties’ representatives. 
 
7. Payment.  Payments shall be made within  30 days of County’s receipt of 
Consultant’s monthly invoices.  Each invoice shall detail the work performed during 
the billing period.  Invoice amounts in pe rcent of Project cost shall not exceed the 
percentage of completion of the Project as approved by County.   
 
8. Defects in Work.  County may reject any work product that fails to meet customary 
professional standards or Project specifi cations.  Consultant agrees to promptly 
remedy all such deficiencies.  The parties shall make a good faith effort to resolve any 
controversy or claim through informal nego tiation as set forth in Section 13.  No 
compensation shall be paid for any reject ed work until such issues have been 
resolved. 
 
9. Insurance Requirements.   
 
A. Consultant shall maintain in effect , at all times during the term of this 
Agreement, insurance adequate to protect County and its agents, 
representatives, officers, officials and employees against such losses as set 
forth below.  Consultant shall provide County with a current Certificate of 
Insurance or a certified copy of the insurance policy naming County as an 
additional insured (except for Errors an d Omissions / Professional Liability 
coverage). 
 
B. The following types an d amounts of insurance are required as minimums: 
 
Worker's Compensation    Statutory 
Professional Liability    $1,000,000 each occurrence

<!-- page 539 -->
Page -3- 
           and annual aggregate 
Consultant’s Protective Bodily Injury  $1,000,000 each occurrence  
           and annual aggregate 
Consultant’s Protective Personal Property $1,000,000 each occurrence  
           and annual aggregate 
Automobile Bodily Injury and Property Damage $1,000,000 each occurrence 
       and annual aggregate 
 
Standard minimum deductibles are a llowed.  Any deductibles are the 
responsibility of Consultant.  Consultant shall immediately inform County of 
any cancellation of insurance or any decrease in the amount of coverage at 
least 30 days before such action takes place.  A violation of this provision may 
be treated as a material breach by Co unty.  Consultant shall notify County 
upon any termination of its regular prof essional liability coverage and shall 
obtain tail coverage for a minimum of five years from the termination date. 
 
C. Consultant acknowledges that the amounts of insurance coverage stated above 
shall not limit its liability under this Agreement. 
 
10. Indemnity.  To the fullest extent allowable  by law, Consultant shall defend, 
indemnify and hold harmless County and its  representatives, officers, officials and 
employees (the “Indemnitees”) from and against any and all claims, damages, losses 
and expenses (including but not limited to attorney fees, court costs, and the costs of 
appellate proceedings) relating to, arising out of or resulting from Consultant’s 
negligent acts, errors, mistakes or omissions in the performance of this Agreement. 
Consultant’s duty to defend, hold harmless and indemnify the Indemnitees shall arise 
in connection with any claim, damage, loss or expense that is attributable to bodily 
injury, sickness, disease, death or injury to, impairment or destruction of property 
including loss of use resulting therefrom, caused in whole or in part by any negligent 
act, error, mistake or omission in the performance of this Agreement (including those 
by any person for whose negligent acts, errors, mistakes or omissions Consultant may 
be liable) to the extent of Consultant’s negligence or fault.  The amount and type of 
insurance coverage requirements set fort h above shall not lim it the scope of the 
indemnity in this paragraph. 
 
11. Records.  Consultant shall retain, and shall require each subcontractor to retain, all 
books, accounts, reports, files and other reco rds relating to this Agreement for a 
period of five years after completion of the work.  All such documents shall be 
subject to inspection and audit by Coun ty upon reasonable notice during normal 
business hours.  A legible co py of any or all such documents shall be produced by 
Consultant at the request and expense of County. 
 
12. Non-Assignment.  Consultant shall not assign any right or interest in this Agreement 
without County’s prior written approval, nor  shall Consultant delegate any duty 
hereunder without County’s prior written approval.   
 
13. Negotiation of Disputes.  The parties shall make a good faith effort to resolve any

<!-- page 540 -->
Page -4- 
claim or controversy or claim through informal negotiation.  Notice of any claim or 
controversy shall be provided in writi ng, with supporting doc umentation, to the 
recipient designated in Section 25.  The recipient shall have seven calendar days to 
prepare and deliver a written response.  If the parties fail to resolve the disputes 
within a reasonable period of not less than ten days, either party may pursue available 
legal remedies. 
 
14. Suspension and Termination by County: 
 
A. Suspension.  County may, without cause, order Consultant in writing to suspend, 
delay or interrupt its performance in whole or part.  An adjustment shall be made 
to the completion date and for any increase in the cost of performance resulting 
from the suspension, delay or interruption.  No adjustment shall be made to the 
extent that performance is or would have been suspended, delayed or interrupted 
by another cause for which Consultant is responsible. 
 
B. Termination for Cause.   County may terminate th is Agreement for cause if 
Consultant refuses or fails to supply enough properly skilled workers to perform 
this Agreement; fails to make required payments to subcontractors; disregards 
laws, ordinances, rules, regulations or  orders of a public  authority having 
jurisdiction; or otherwise commits a material breach of  this Agreement.  When 
any of the foregoing causes exist, County shall give Consultant written notice and 
a ten-day opportunity to cure the default.  If the default remains uncured, County 
may (without prejudice to any other rights or remedies it may have) terminate this 
Agreement and finish the work or cause it to be finished.  If the unpaid balance of 
the Agreement exceeds the cost of fini shing the work (including any expenses 
incurred by County as a result of Cons ultant’s default), Consultant shall be 
entitled to payment for its performance up to the amount of such excess.  If the 
cost of finishing the work exceeds the u npaid balance, Consultant shall pay the 
difference to County. 
 
C. Termination for Convenience.   County, by written n otice to Consultant, may 
terminate this Agreement in whole or part when in the sole discretion of County it 
is in County’s best interests to do so.  Consultant shall be pa id for all material, 
equipment and services provided, as well as reasonable termination expenses and 
a reasonable allowance for profit and overhead, provided that such payments, 
exclusive of termination expenses, shal l not exceed the total amount payable 
pursuant to Section 6 less any paymen ts previously made to Consultant.  
Consultant shall not be entitled to profit and overhead on material, equipment and 
services which were not provided. 
 
D. Consultant’s Responsibilities.  Upon receipt of a termination notice, Consultant 
shall (a) promptly discontinue all servic es (unless the notice directs otherwise), 
and (b) deliver or otherwise make available to County copies of all data, design 
calculations, drawings, specifications, reports, estimates, summaries and other 
information and materials developed or accumulated by Consultant in performing 
this Agreement.

<!-- page 541 -->
Page -5- 
 
15. Termination by Consultant.  Consultant may terminate this Agreement upon written 
notice to County if performance is made impossible for a period of 30 consecutive 
calendar days for any of the following reasons through no act or fault of Consultant or 
its agents, employees or subcontractors: issuance of an order by a court or other 
public authority having jurisdiction; an act  of government, such as a declaration of 
national emergency; or a natural disaster or other Act of God.  Consultant may also 
terminate this Agreement upon written notice  and a ten-day opportunity to cure if 
County fails to make any payment within the time set forth in Section 7.  Consultant 
may also terminate this Ag reement upon written notice if  suspensions, delays or 
interruptions by County equal in the aggregate more than 100% of the total number of 
days scheduled for completion. 
 
16. Governing Law.   This Agreement shall  be governed by the law of the State  of 
Arizona.  Any suit arising out of this Agreement shall be brought in the state courts of 
Arizona (with venue in Navajo County) or the federal District of Arizona, but only 
after informal negotiation pursuant to Section 13. 
 
17. Incorporation of Applicable Laws.  Every provision of law required by statute or 
regulation to be in this Agreement will be read and enforced as though included 
herein.  Each party shall promptly notify the other upon discovery that any such 
provision has been omitted. 
 
18. Non-Collusion.  The provisions of A.R.S. § 38-511 are incorporated herein by this 
reference. 
 
19. One-Year Limitation on Actions.  No action shall be maintained by Consultant on 
any claim based upon or aris ing out of this Agreement unless such action is 
commenced within one year after County’s final payment hereunder. 
 
20. Term.  This Contract for Professional Service is valid for one (1) year from the date 
the agreement was made between the two parties. Upon mutual agreement between 
the parties, this contract may be renewed up to (4) four additional one-year terms. 
 
21. Entire Agreement.  This Agreement and those documents incorporated by reference 
represent the entire understanding between  the parties.  No amendment shall be 
effective unless executed by both parties with the same formality as this Agreement.  
Provided, however, that the County’s project  manager is authorized to modify the 
Scope of Work, in writing, with the concurrence of Consultant so long as the project 
is not changed substantially or significant additional compensation is not required. 
 
22. Prohibition of Boycott of Israel:  Contractor certifies th at neither it nor its 
subcontractors are currently or will be engaged in, for the duration of this Contract 
engage in, a boycott of Israel as defined by A.R.S. § 35-393 et seq.  Violation of this 
certification may result in an action by the County up to and including termination of 
the Contract.
