NAVAJO COUNTY 
CONTRACT FOR PUBLIC HEALTH MEDICAL CONSULTATION/ALTERNATIVE MEDICAL 
EXAMINER SERVICES WITH PUBLIC HEALTH PARTNERS, LLC, 
WADE KARTCHNER, M.D., MPH, DIRECTOR 
 
AGREEMENT made between Navajo County, Arizona, a political subdivision of the State of Arizona, 
("County"), and Public Health Partners, LLC ("Independent Contractor") effective January 1, 2023 to renew 
and to terminate on January 31, 2023.  This is an extension of the existing contract to ensure no disruption 
to Medical Director and Medical Examiner services with completing the RFP process and securing and 
putting into place a new contract for services. 
 
WHEREAS, Navajo County has requested Public Health Medical Consultant/Alternative Medical 
Examiner services ("Project"); and 
 
WHEREAS, Independent Contractor represents that it has the necessary expertise to provide 
such services in accordance with the terms of this Agreement. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the parties agree as follows: 
 
1. Services. Independent Contractor shall perform the work described below in a competent and 
professional manner to the satisfaction of County. 
 
a. Public Health Medical Consultant duties include: 
 
• Developing and evaluating standards of care for medical care throughout the Nursing 
Department. 
• Develop and update standing medical orders for the public health nursing staff. 
• Act as the provider of record on all Insurance Contracts. 
• Provide medical and epidemiological consultation for routine disease surveillance and 
investigations into communicable disease outbreaks. 
• Provide medical consultation for any animal control issues, particularly rabies 
• Provide medical consultation for vital records. 
• Provide medical consultation for public health emergency preparedness. 
• Provide medical and public health consultation during public health emergences. 
• Act as the provider of record for the Center for Disease Control's chempack. 
• Provide medical consultation for environmental health. 
• Provide medical consultation for the International Travel Health Clinic. 
             Provider of record for the Yellow Fever clinic. 
 Serve as Tuberculosis Control medical consultant. 
 
b. Alternative Medical Examiner duties include: 
 
• In consultation with the Health Director, assists in formulating and implementing policy for 
the operation of the office. 
• Works with the Medicolegal Death Investigators on all cases referred to the office. 
• Remotely supervises external examinations when indicated. 
• Performs record reviews when indicated. 
• Directs the Medicolegal Death Investigators to take indicated cases to autopsy. 
• Authorizes all cremations within Navajo County. 
 
2. Independent Contractor's Expertise. Independent Contractor warrants that it has the ability, 
authority, capacity and professional expertise to perform this Agreement. 
 
3. Independent Contractor. Independent Contractor acknowledges that it is an independent 
contractor.  Neither  party is  or  shall purport to be an agent, employee, partner,  joint  venture or

<!-- page 550 -->
2  
associate of the other. An employee or agent of one party shall not be deemed an employer or agent 
of the other party for any purpose. Taxes or Social Security contributions will not be withheld from 
any payment by County, and Independent Contractor shall be solely responsible for such matters. 
 
4. Subcontracts.  Independent Contractor shall not enter into any subcontract with respect to any of 
the work to be performed hereunder without County's prior written approval. All subcontracts shall 
comply with applicable federal and state laws and regulations and shall impose on the subcontractor 
substantially the same obligations as are imposed. on Independent Contractor by  this  Agreement  
with  respect  to  those  matters  covered  by Sections  8, 9,  1o,  11,  14  and 17. 
_Independent  Contractor  is  responsible  for  full  performance  of  this  Agreement  regardless  of 
whether subcontractors are used. 
 
5. Term of Contract. The term of this Contract shall commence upon the execution of this Agreement 
and shall continue for a period of One Year thereafter, unless terminated, cancelled or extended as 
otherwise provided herein. This Agreement is renewable on an annual basis if both parties mutually 
agree to the renewal of the Agreement, and continuation of this Agreement. 
 
6. Contract Price. County shall pay Independent Contractor an amount of $2,000.00 BI- MONTHLY, 
on the 15th and 30th of the month, for services provided as the Public Health Medical Consultant for 
the duration of this Contract for services provided under this Contract; and shall make an additional 
payment to the  Independent Contractor for services  provided in the capacity as the Alternative  
Medical Examiner. The Independent  Contractor  shall be paid in the amount of 
$500.00 for each external exam remotely supervised by the Independent Contractor, $400.00 for 
each record review performed by the Independent Contractor, and $25.00 for each cremation 
authorization performed by the Independent Contractor. 
 
7. Payment. Contractor shall submit all invoices to the County Health Director of the County Health 
Department for Navajo County. Upon approval of the County Health Director, payment will be made 
by County within thirty (30) days after approval. Each invoice shall detail the work performed during 
the billing period. If the County fails to make any payment due the Independent Contractor for 
services  and expenses  within 30 days after receipt  of   Independent  Contractor's 
invoice,  the   amount  due   to  the  Independent   Contractor  will  be increased  at a rate of 1 % per 
month from  said  thirtieth day.

A. Independent Contractor shall maintain in effect, at all times during the term of this Agreement, 
insurance adequate to protect the County (and their respective officers and employees, 
participants and equipment funded under this Agreement) against such losses as set f orth 
below.  Independent  Contractor shall provide County with a current Certificate of Insurance or 
a certified copy of the insurance policy naming County as additional insured( except for Errors 
and Omissions coverage). 
 
B. The following types and amounts of insurance are required as minimums:

10. Non•Assignment. Independent Contractor shall not assign any right or interest in this 
Agreement without County's prior written approval, nor shall Independent Contractor delegate 
any duty hereunder without County's prior written approval. 
 
11. Claims and Disputes. All claims, disputes , and matters in question between the County and  
Independent Contractor arising out of this Agreement, which are not resolved to the satisfaction 
of either of the parties, will be decided through alternative dispute resolution prior to proceeding to 
litigation in Court. Failure to complete the review procedure specified herein shall be a complete  
bar to any legal action for any quest ion which could have been submitted to an alternative dispute 
resolution proceeding for determination. 
 
12. Suspension and Termination by County: 
 
A. Suspension. County may, without cause, order Independent Contractor in writing to  suspend, 
delay or interrupt its performance in whole or part. A payment adjustment shall be made for any 
services not provided resulting from the suspension, delay or interruption. 
 
B. Termination for  Cause . County may terminate this Agreement for cause if Independent 
Contractor fails to have certification; is arrested or convicted of any criminal offense 
misdemeanor or felony which in the opinion of the County impairs Independent Contractor in 
performance of its duties; disregards laws, ordinances, rules, regulations or orders of the 
County or a public authority having jurisdiction; or otherwise commits a material breach of this 
Agreement. When any of the foregoing   causes exist,   County   shall give Independent  
Contractor written notice and a ten-day opportunity to cure the default.   If the default remains 
uncured, County may (without prejudice to any other rights or remedies it may have) terminate 
this Agreement.

c. Termination for Convenience. County, by written notice to Independent Contractor, may

provided, as well as reasonable termination expenses, provided that such payments,

D. Independent Contractor's Responsibilities. Upon receipt of a termination notice,

and other information and materials developed or accumulated by Independent Contractor in

upon written notice to County if performance is made impossible fora period of 30 consecutive 
calendar days for any of the following reasons through no act or fault of Independent Contractor or 
its agents, employees or subcontractors: issuance of an order by  a court or other public authority 
having jurisdiction; an act of government, such as a declaration of national emergency; or a natural 
disaster or other Act of God. Independent Contractor may also term inate this Agreement, upon 
written notice and a ten-day opportunity to cure, if the County fails to fulfill its obligations or to make 
any payment within the time set forth in Section 6. 
 
Termination for Convenience. Independent Contractor, by providing sixty (60) day written notice 
to County, may terminate this Agreement in whole or part when in the sole discretion of Independent 
Contractor, it is in Independent Contractor's best interests to do so.

attorney's fees and costs.

NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT 
PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
THIS AGREEMENT is made and entered into between the Navajo County Public Health 
Services District (the "District"), a political subdivision of the State of Arizona, and 
Jaskiranjit Hanson, MSN, FNP-BC (the "Subcontractor"). 
 
WHEREAS, the District requires the services of a  consultant nurse practitioner to assist 
in the fulfillment of the District's contracts with the Arizona Department of Health Services 
(ADHS) for Family Planning Services, STD Services, HIV Services, and Immunization 
Services. Services are also required for billable services as the provider of record for 
diagnosis, treatment and prescribing. 
WHEREAS, the Subcontractor is a nurse practitioner licensed to practice in Arizona; and 
WHEREAS, the District wishes to contract with the Subcontractor for reproductive health, 
STI, HIV & immunizations consulting and physical examination services as described 
herein, and the Subcontractor is willing to provide such services upon the terms and 
conditions set forth herein, 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the parties agree as follows: 
1. Term. This Agreement shall commence as of February  1, 202 3 (the "Effective 
Date") and shall automatically renew annually, up to four additional one-year terms, 
on February 1, unless requests to amend or terminate are submitted in writing by 
either party at least 60 days prior to the February 1 renewal date. 
 
2.  Services. The Subcontractor shall provide various health consulting and physical 
examination services and shall sign routine standing orders and review medical 
protocols related to immunizations, family planning, STI, and HIV; all in accordance 
with the ADHS Contracts, and under the general direction of the Medical Director 
of the District. The Subcontractor acknowledges receipt of a copy of the ADHS 
Contracts and understands and agrees that all services hereunder shall be 
provided in strict compliance with  the ADHS Contracts. All services shall be 
provided in accordance with the professional and ethical standards applicable to  
Registered Nurses and Certified Nurse Practitioners, and within the contractor's 
legal scope of practice.

<!-- page 558 -->
The Subcontractor shall act as the provider named in various contracts with 
Medicare, Medicaid, and Private Insurances for billable public health services 
relevant to immunizations, family planning, STD, and HIV. The Subcontractor shall 
work with various insurance, billing, and laboratory  subcontractors of  Navajo 
County Public Health Services District  to ensure appropriate credentialing is in 
place, and up-to-date. 
 
Services for reproductive health shall  be provided two and a half days per month 
(two full days at the district's Show Low office, and one -half day in the district's 
Holbrook office; or as agreed upon with the Navajo County Public Health Services 
District Director). Consultation and physical examination for immunization, STI & 
HIV services will  be available on an as  needed basis during these hours, and 
remotely during other times throughout the month. The parties shall agree upon a 
mutually satisfactory schedule, which can be adjusted by either party as needed. 
 
3.  Compensation. As full and complete compensation for the services to be 
provided hereunder, the District shall pay to the Subcontractor a monthly fee of 
$3,500 ($3,000 per month from the Family Planning grant, and $500 per month 
from the health district). 
 
Family Planning nurse practitioner services 
STI activities (partner services, testing, treatment) 
Laboratory, STD, HIV & Immunization Orders, & Insurance 
Billable Services, STI consultation and risk reduction planning 
Totaling 
$3,000.00 
$ 200.00 
$ 300.00 
$3,500.00 
 
At the beginning of the first month that this Agreement is in effect, and at the 
beginning of each month thereafter, the contractor will invoice Navajo County 
Public Health for the hours worked.  The Public Health Nursing Supervisor will 
submit to Navajo County a demand in the amount of the monthly fee.  Payment 
shall be made directly to the Subcontractor, in accordance with the County's 
standard procedures for processing demands. 
4. Independent Contractor. In providing services hereunder, the Subcontractor is 
an independent contractor. The  Subcontractor shall not be deemed an employee 
of the District and shall not be entitled to any benefits provided to the District 
Employees. Taxes, Social Security and other amounts customarily withheld from 
the earnings of employees shall not be withheld from the compensation paid to the 
Subcontractor. 
5. Insurance. The Subcontractor shall maintain in force during the term of this 
Agreement, at the Subcontractor's expense, professional liability insurance, in the 
amount of no less than $1,000,000 per  occurrence, and such other insurance as 
the District's Risk Manager may reasonably require. The Subcontractor shall 
provide the District with certificates of insurance evidencing all required policies

<!-- page 559 -->
and shall notify the District of any cancellation or decrease in the amount of 
coverage at least 30 days before the effective date of such cancellation or 
decrease. 
 
6. Compliance with Laws. The Subcontractor shall comply with all federal and state 
statutes, regulations and orders applicable to the services provided hereunder. All 
federal and state laws required to be incorporated into the Agreement shall be 
enforced as though fully set forth herein. The Subcontractor will work with in their 
professional scope of practice and be expected to understand what those 
limitations are. 
7. Professional Licenses. The Subcontractor shall maintain in force throughout the 
term of this Agreement any and all licenses, permits and accreditations required 
for the Subcontractor to provide services hereunder. The Subcontractor shall notify 
the District and shall immediately cease performance hereunder if any such license, 
permit or accreditation is suspended or revoked.  These licenses must include at 
minimum a valid RN or NP license, current CPR certification, current fingerprint 
clearance and current PPD skin test. 
 
8. Reports and Records. The record on a client at the time of physical examination 
and all other client records shall be retained in the District Office. The 
Subcontractor is familiar with all state, and department record -retention and 
confidentiality requirements set forth in the ADHS Contract and applicable federal 
and state laws and shall strictly comply with all such requirements in handling client 
records and information. 
9. Indemnification. To the fullest extent allowed by law, each party (as "lndemnitor") 
shall indemnify, defend and hold harmless the other party and its agents, 
representatives, officers, officials and employees (the "lndemnitees")  from and 
against any and all claims, damages, losses and expenses (including but not 
limited to attorney fees, court costs and the costs of appellate proceedings) relating 
to, arising out of or resulting from the lndemnitor's negligent acts, errors, mistakes 
or omissions in the per formance of this Agreement.  The lndemnitor's negligent 
acts, errors, mistakes or omissions in the performance of this Agreement.  The 
lndemnitor's duty to defend, hold harmless and indemnify the lndemnitees shall 
arise in connection with any claim, damage, loss or expense that is attributable to 
bodily injury, sickness, disease, death or injury to any person, or impairment or 
destruction of property including loss of use resulting  therefrom, caused in whole 
or in part by any negligent act, error, mistake or omission in the performance of 
this Agreement (including those by any person for whose negligent acts, errors, 
mistakes or omissions the lndemnitor may be liable). 
 
10. Termination. This Agreement shall terminate immediately and automatically upon 
the termination of the ADHS Contract for any reason. In addition, either party may 
terminate this Agreement for any reason upon 60 days prior written

<!-- page 560 -->
notice to the other party.  In addition, the District may terminate this Agreement 
upon written notice if the Subcontractor fails to cure any default in performance 
within ten days after delivery of a written notice of default by the District. This 
Agreement is also subject to cancellation pursuant to ARS §§ 38-511 (concerning 
conflicts of interest). 
 
11.  Non-Assignment. The Subcontractor shall not assign any right or interest in this 
Agreement without the District's prior written approval, nor shall the Subcontractor 
delegate or subcontract any duty hereunder without the District's prior written 
approval. Any purported assignment, delegati on or subcontract without the 
District's prior written approval shall be void.

Board of Supervisors Regular 6. b. 1.        
Meeting Date: 01/09/2024  
Title: Access and Functional Needs Operating Plan, Contract No. B23-11-013 
Submitted For: Catrina Jenkins, EMP Director 
Submitted By: Cheryl Hollins, Account Specialist
Department: NC Public Health Services District Division: Emergency Management
Presented By: Catrina Jenkins / Kymberle
O'Farrell
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of award of contract B23-11-013 Access and Functional Needs Operating Plan
to Connect Consulting Services.
Background:
This contract is to retain a consultant to provide an Access and Functional Needs Operations
Plan: An Annex to the Navajo County Emergency Operations Plan will be a detailed plan that
encompasses the policies of the County, State and Federal authorities with respect to all hazard
emergency planning, including a pandemic for citizens with access and functional (AFN) needs
within Navajo County including tribal lands
Sealed proposals were received by the Board of Supervisors at the Navajo County
Governmental Center, Holbrook, Arizona until 3:00 p.m., M.S.T, December 28, 2023.  No
proposals were accepted after 3:00 p.m.  The proposals were publicly opened and read aloud at
3:15 p.m.  One proposal was received and evaluated per the criteria set forth in the request for
proposals.
Recommendation: 
The Evaluation Committee is recommending award of Contract B23-11-013 Access and
Functional Needs Operating Plan to Connect Consulting Services.

 CONTRACT FOR PROFESSIONAL SERVICES  
 
AGREEMENT made as of January 9, 2024, between Navajo County ("County") and 
Connect Consulting Services. (“Consultant”). 
 
WHEREAS, County requires certain professional services for Navajo County Access 
and Functional Needs Operating Plan, Contract No. B23-11-013 (“Project”); and 
 
WHEREAS, Consultant represents that it has the necessary expertise to provide such 
services in accordance with the terms of this Agreement. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the parties agree as follows: 
 
1. Services.  Consultant shall perform the work  described in the Proposal dated 
December 27, 2023, and those ad ditional items set forth in  the Consultant’s Cost 
Breakdown in Part B, Sect ion 5 of the proposal da ted December 27, 2023, in a 
competent and professional manner to the satisfaction of County.  The Consultant’s 
Proposal is attached hereto and by this reference incorporated herein. 
 
2. Contract Documents.
 The Contract Documents comprise this Contract, Request for 
Proposals No. B23-11-013, Proposal dated December 27, 2023, and addendums 
thereto, if any. If any incorporated term is inconsistent with the Contract, this 
Contract shall control over all others; the Request for Proposal shall control over the 
Consultants proposal. 
 
3. Consultant’s Expertise.  Consultant warrants that it has the ability, authority, 
capacity and professional expertise to perfo rm this Agreement.  Consultant shall 
provide an adequate staff of experienced  personnel, capable of and devoted to the 
successful accomplishment of the work to be performed hereunder.  Consultant shall 
assign specific individuals to key positio ns.  Once assigned to work under this 
Agreement, key personnel shall not be removed or replaced without County’s prior 
written approval.  
 
4. Independent Contractor.  Consultant acknowledges that  it is an independent 
contractor.  Neither party is or shall purport to be an agent, employee, partner, joint 
venturer or associate of the other.  An employee or agen t of one party shall not be 
deemed an employer or agent  of the other party for any purpose.  Taxes or Social 
Security contributions will not be w ithheld from any paym ent by County, and 
Consultant shall be solely responsible for such matters.

<!-- page 568 -->
Page -2- 
5. Subcontracts.  Consultant shall not enter into any subcontract with respect to any of 
the work to be performed hereunder without County’s prior written approval.  All 
subcontracts shall comply with applicable federal and state laws and regulations and 
shall impose on the subcontractor substantially the same obligations as are imposed 
on Consultant by this Agreement with respect to those matters covered by Sections 9, 
10, 11, 13, 16, 19, 22 and 23.  Consultant is responsible for full performance of this 
Agreement regardless of whether subcontractors are used. 
 
6. Time for Completion.  Consultant’s services will be provided in a manner consistent 
with the degree of care and skill ordi narily exercised by me mbers of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances.  Consultant’s services 
shall be performed as expeditiously as is consistent with professional skill and care 
and the orderly progress of the Project, and in accord ance with an agreed upon 
schedule for the performance of the Services hereunder.  Time limits established by a 
schedule approved by County shall not, except for reasonable cause, be exceeded by 
County or Consultant.  The Schedule shall be adjusted, if  necessary, as the Project 
proceeds.  The date for final performance shall be extended by the number of days 
that performance is delayed by governmental approval or review procedures or other 
causes beyond Consultant’s reasonable control, as jointly confirmed in writing by the 
parties’ representatives. 
 
7. Payment.  County shall pay to C onsultant a not-to-exceed  sum of eighty-three 
thousand four hundred fifteen dollars ($83,415.00) as payment in full for all services 
rendered by Consultant pursuant to this Agreement.  Payments shall be made within 
30 days of County’s receipt of Consultant’s monthly invoices.  Each invoice shall 
detail the work performed during the billing period.  Invoice amounts in percent of 
Project cost shall not exceed the percentage of completion of the Project as approved 
by County.   
 
8. Defects in Work.  County may reject any work product that fails to meet customary 
professional standards or Project specifi cations.  Consultant agrees to promptly 
remedy all such deficiencies.  The parties shall make a good faith effort to resolve any 
controversy or claim through informal nego tiation as set forth in Section 13.  No 
compensation shall be paid for any reject ed work until such issues have been 
resolved. 
 
9. Insurance Requirements.   
 
A. Consultant shall maintain in effect , at all times during the term of this 
Agreement, insurance adequate to protect County and its agents, 
representatives, officers, officials and employees against such losses as set 
forth below.  Consultant shall provide County with a current Certificate of 
Insurance or a certified copy of the insurance policy naming County as an 
additional insured (except for Errors an d Omissions / Professional Liability 
coverage). 
 
B. The following types an d amounts of insurance are required as minimums:

<!-- page 569 -->
Page -3- 
 
Worker's Compensation    Statutory 
Professional Liability    $1,000,000 each occurrence 
           and annual aggregate 
Consultant’s Protective Bodily Injury  $1,000,000 each occurrence  
           and annual aggregate 
Consultant’s Protective Personal Property $1,000,000 each occurrence  
           and annual aggregate 
Automobile Bodily Injury and Property Damage $1,000,000 each occurrence 
       and annual aggregate 
 
Standard minimum deductibles are a llowed.  Any deductibles are the 
responsibility of Consultant.  Consultant shall immediately inform County of 
any cancellation of insurance or any decrease in the amount of coverage at 
least 30 days before such action takes place.  A violation of this provision may 
be treated as a material breach by Co unty.  Consultant shall notify County 
upon any termination of its regular prof essional liability coverage and shall 
obtain tail coverage for a minimum of five years from the termination date. 
 
C. Consultant acknowledges that the amounts of insurance coverage stated above 
shall not limit its liability under this Agreement. 
 
10. Indemnity.  To the fullest extent allowable  by law, Consultant shall defend, 
indemnify and hold harmless County and its  representatives, officers, officials and 
employees (the “Indemnitees”) from and against any and all claims, damages, losses 
and expenses (including but not limited to attorney fees, court costs, and the costs of 
appellate proceedings) relating to, arising out of or resulting from Consultant’s 
negligent acts, errors, mistakes or omissions in the performance of this Agreement. 
Consultant’s duty to defend, hold harmless and indemnify the Indemnitees shall arise 
in connection with any claim, damage, loss or expense that is attributable to bodily 
injury, sickness, disease, death or injury to, impairment or destruction of property 
including loss of use resulting therefrom, caused in whole or in part by any negligent 
act, error, mistake or omission in the performance of this Agreement (including those 
by any person for whose negligent acts, errors, mistakes or omissions Consultant may 
be liable) to the extent of Consultant’s negligence or fault.  The amount and type of 
insurance coverage requirements set fort h above shall not lim it the scope of the 
indemnity in this paragraph. 
 
11. Records.  Consultant shall retain, and shall require each subcontractor to retain, all 
books, accounts, reports, files and other reco rds relating to this Agreement for a 
period of five years after completion of the work.  All such documents shall be 
subject to inspection and audit by Coun ty upon reasonable notice during normal 
business hours.  A legible co py of any or all such documents shall be produced by 
Consultant at the request and expense of County. 
 
12. Non-Assignment.  Consultant shall not assign any right or interest in this Agreement 
without County’s prior written approval, nor  shall Consultant delegate any duty

<!-- page 570 -->
Page -4- 
hereunder without County’s prior written approval.   
 
13. Negotiation of Disputes.  The parties shall make a good faith effort to resolve any 
claim or controversy or claim through informal negotiation.  Notice of any claim or 
controversy shall be provided in writi ng, with supporting doc umentation, to the 
recipient designated in Section 25.  The recipient shall have seven calendar days to 
prepare and deliver a written response.  If the parties fail to resolve the disputes 
within a reasonable period of not less than ten days, either party may pursue available 
legal remedies. 
 
14. Suspension and Termination by County: 
 
A. Suspension.  County may, without cause, order Consultant in writing to suspend, 
delay or interrupt its performance in whole or part.  An adjustment shall be made 
to the completion date and for any increase in the cost of performance resulting 
from the suspension, delay or interruption.  No adjustment shall be made to the 
extent that performance is or would have been suspended, delayed or interrupted 
by another cause for which Consultant is responsible. 
 
B. Termination for Cause.   County may terminate th is Agreement for cause if 
Consultant refuses or fails to supply enough properly skilled workers to perform 
this Agreement; fails to make required payments to subcontractors; disregards 
laws, ordinances, rules, regulations or  orders of a public  authority having 
jurisdiction; or otherwise commits a material breach of  this Agreement.  When 
any of the foregoing causes exist, County shall give Consultant written notice and 
a ten-day opportunity to cure the default.  If the default remains uncured, County 
may (without prejudice to any other rights or remedies it may have) terminate this 
Agreement and finish the work or cause it to be finished.  If the unpaid balance of 
the Agreement exceeds the cost of fini shing the work (including any expenses 
incurred by County as a result of Cons ultant’s default), Consultant shall be 
entitled to payment for its performance up to the amount of such excess.  If the 
cost of finishing the work exceeds the u npaid balance, Consultant shall pay the 
difference to County. 
 
C. Termination for Convenience.   County, by written n otice to Consultant, may 
terminate this Agreement in whole or part when in the sole discretion of County it 
is in County’s best interests to do so.  Consultant shall be pa id for all material, 
equipment and services provided, as well as reasonable termination expenses and 
a reasonable allowance for profit and overhead, provided that such payments, 
exclusive of termination expenses, shal l not exceed the total amount payable 
pursuant to Section 6 less any paymen ts previously made to Consultant.  
Consultant shall not be entitled to profit and overhead on material, equipment and 
services which were not provided. 
 
D. Consultant’s Responsibilities.  Upon receipt of a termination notice, Consultant 
shall (a) promptly discontinue all servic es (unless the notice directs otherwise), 
and (b) deliver or otherwise make available to County copies of all data, design

<!-- page 571 -->
Page -5- 
calculations, drawings, specifications, reports, estimates, summaries and other 
information and materials developed or accumulated by Consultant in performing 
this Agreement. 
 
15. Termination by Consultant.  Consultant may terminate this Agreement upon written 
notice to County if performance is made impossible for a period of 30 consecutive 
calendar days for any of the following reasons through no act or fault of Consultant or 
its agents, employees or subcontractors: issuance of an order by a court or other 
public authority having jurisdiction; an act  of government, such as a declaration of 
national emergency; or a natural disaster or other Act of God.  Consultant may also 
terminate this Agreement upon written notice  and a ten-day opportunity to cure if 
County fails to make any payment within the time set forth in Section 7.  Consultant 
may also terminate this Ag reement upon written notice if  suspensions, delays or 
interruptions by County equal in the aggregate more than 100% of the total number of 
days scheduled for completion. 
 
16. Governing Law.   This Agreement shall  be governed by the law of the State  of 
Arizona.  Any suit arising out of this Agreement shall be brought in the state courts of 
Arizona (with venue in Navajo County) or the federal District of Arizona, but only 
after informal negotiation pursuant to Section 13. 
 
17. Incorporation of Applicable Laws.  Every provision of law required by statute or 
regulation to be in this Agreement will be read and enforced as though included 
herein.  Each party shall promptly notify the other upon discovery that any such 
provision has been omitted. 
 
18. Non-Collusion.  The provisions of A.R.S. § 38-511 are incorporated herein by this 
reference. 
 
19. One-Year Limitation on Actions.  No action shall be maintained by Consultant on 
any claim based upon or aris ing out of this Agreement unless such action is 
commenced within one year after County’s final payment hereunder. 
 
20. Term.  This Contract for Professional Service is valid for five and one half months 
from the date the agreement was made between the two parties.  
 
21. Entire Agreement.  This Agreement and those documents incorporated by reference 
represent the entire understanding between  the parties.  No amendment shall be 
effective unless executed by both parties with the same formality as this Agreement.  
Provided, however, that the County’s project  manager is authorized to modify the 
Scope of Work, in writing, with the concurrence of Consultant so long as the project 
is not changed substantially or significant additional compensation is not required. 
 
22. Prohibition of Boycott of Israel:  Contractor certifies th at neither it nor its 
subcontractors are currently or will be engaged in, for the duration of this Contract 
engage in, a boycott of Israel as defined by A.R.S. § 35-393 et seq.  Violation of this 
certification may result in an action by the County up to and including termination of

Contractor certifies that it is not currently  using, and agrees for the duration of this

Board of Supervisors Regular 7. a.        
Meeting Date: 01/09/2024  
Title: ABAN 23-02 Stub Road In Scotts Pine Meadows Subdivision: District IV
Submitted For: Bill Bess, County Engineer/Assistant Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Bryan Cook, Sr. Engineering Project Manager II
Department: Public Works
Presented By: Bill Bess
Motion before the Board:
Consideration and adoption of Resolution Number 01-2024, granting a Right-of-Way
Abandonment of an unused stub road between lots 214 & 215 and North from the northern edge
of right-of way for Wild Game Trail. 
Background:
Navajo County Public Works received an abandonment request for a stump road off Wild Game
Trail in Scotts Pine Meadows Subdivision from David Thompson (Applicant), owner of parcel
number 212-10-104. This stub road is not developed  and was intended originally to allow
connection to an at the time undeveloped subdivision. However, when the adjoining subdivision
was developed, it was developed as a private development where no connection to the stub road
was created and a parcel was created instead. The subdivision does not have a Home Owners
Association (HOA). This stub road has two directly impacted property owners, the Applicant to
the west and George Hamann to the east for parcel number 212-10-105.
With no potential public use or access need for the stub road to provide a connection, it is our
opinion that there is no public need for this section of roadway. This position was also
supported when an improvement district was established to pave the roads; the stub road was
not included in the improvement.
This agenda request is for the willingness to abandon the public right-of-way and the support of
the board to allow the sail of the property to the adjacent land owners as prescribed in ARS
§28-7204. Additional requirements will need to be met prior to the selling of the property
should the abandonment request be approved. These include the applicant going through a plat
amendment process, providing a survey grade legal description of both sections of the stub road
once split, having the quiet claim deeds created, and having the property appraised for fair
market value. The Board's willingness to sell the property will prevent unnecessary expenditure
while meeting stipulations prior to the actual approval and authorization for the Chair to sign
the quitclaim deed.
Once the stipulations have been met, the sale of the property under ARS §28-7204 with
additional notices and postings will occur. The Board of Supervisors will then determine if the
proposed fair market value is acceptable, potentially approve the sale and authorize the chair to

<!-- page 576 -->
sign the quiet claim deeds.
For this abandonment, should all stipulations be met by the applicant, the two adjacent property
owners to the stub road, within the Scotts Pine Meadows Subdivision, would have preference to
each acquire half of the abandonment, a fifty-fifty split down the centerline as prescribed in
ARS §28-7205.  The Board of Supervisors can also make the determination of fair market value
should they so choose and take into consideration that less than 1 tenth of an acre of land is
shaped into a narrow strip for each portion to be acquired by each property owner in accordance
with ARS §28-7208. Additionally, take into account that no public funds have been expended
on the acquisition and or for any maintenance.
During the 60-day notification window from when letters were sent out to the individual
property owners, and certified letters sent to the directly affected property owners. Staff did not
receive any calls in protest, support, or requesting additional information. 
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends the approval of the motion for the abandonment and to authorize the Chair to
sign the resolution.

RESOLUTION NO._____-23 
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS  
GRANTING A RIGHT-OF-WAY ABANDONMENT (ABAN 23-02) OF A  
UNUSED STUB ROAD BETWEEN LOTS 214 & 215 
 RECORDED IN PLAT BOOK 8 PAGE 12 
 
WHEREAS, the Board of Supervisors of Navajo County is authorized by Section 28-6701 and 28-6709 of 
the Arizona Revised Statutes to abandon pubic roadways that are no longer necessary for public use; and 
WHEREAS, the abandonment is for an unused stub road between Lots 214 and 215 and north from the 
northern edge of right-of-way for Wild Game Trail as dedicate in Plat Book 8 page 12; and 
WHEREAS, Lot 214 and Lot 215 each have an equal split of the abandonment to the centerline of the stub 
road should they both decide to participate and provide fair market value for the abandonment sections ; and 
WHEREAS, both adjacent lot owners have been notified of the abandonment and their preference 
position to acquire the land for fair market value, one being the applicant and the other through certified mail.  
WHEREAS, the application has been vetted by staff and determined that no parcels will be landlocked, 
that the right-of-way is not needed for public benefit, the portion to be abandoned is not part of Navajo County’s 
Maintenance System; and 
WHEREAS, the application has been reviewed by the Deputy County Attorney and Navajo County has the 
legal capacity and authority to abandon public right-of-way; and 
 WHEREAS, the abandonment is for public right-of-way only and does not alter, change, or eliminate the 
any underlaying easement for utilities per ARS §28-7210 
WHEREAS, the Board of Supervisors, having heard public comment and after due consideration for those 
facts presented; find that with consideration of public safety, health, general welfare and convenience warrant the 
following action; 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that by the Board of Supervisors of Navajo County; does hereby 
Grant a right-of-way abandonment as stated in this resolution with the following stipulation: 
o The applicant will, at their own expense, acquire a plat amendment correcting the stub road to 
be private property that is dependent on the completion and acceptance of the fair market 
value for the abandonment. 
o The applicant will, at their own expense, retain an appraiser acceptable to Navajo County to 
appraise the abandoned area for fair market value as equal halves.  
o The applicant will, at their own expense, retain professional services to create a legal 
descriptions and quit claim deeds for both halves of the stub road that is being abandoned. 
o Together with the quit claim deeds, legal descriptions, and an agreed upon appraised value will 
be used by the Board of Supervisors to approve the sale of a portion of public roadway  by 
resolution pursuant to ARS § 28-6701, 6709, and 7204.  
o The non-applicant adjacent property owner will be entitled to the work performed by the 
applicant for the appraisal, preparation of legal description and any costs associated with the

<!-- page 578 -->
plat amendment, but not the fair market acquisition of the property. If the adjacent property 
owner declines to participate, both halves can be acquired by the applicant.   
o Should the above requirements not be met within one (1) year time from the recording of this 
resolution due to inaction or otherwise loss of interest in proceeding. This resolution will be 
void and the right-of-way will be once again considered as public property. 
PASSED AND ADOPTED on ________, 2023 at Holbrook, Arizona.

CONTRACT FOR PROFESSIONAL SERVICES 
 
 
AGREEMENT made as of December 21, 2023, between Navajo County Flood 
Control District ("District") and The WLB Group, Inc. (“Consultant”). 
 
WHEREAS, District requires certain professional services for Bourdon Ranch Road / 
Millet Swale Culverts – Construction Administration and Easements (“Project”); and 
 
WHEREAS, Consultant represents that it has the necessary expertise to provide such 
services in accordance with the terms of this Agreement. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the parties agree as follows: 
 
1. Services.  Consultant shall perform the work described in the Scope of Work dated 
December 19, 2023, and those additional items set forth in the Consultant’s schedule 
and fee proposal dated December 19, 2023, in a competent and professional manner 
to the satisfaction of District.  The Scope of Work and the Consultant’s schedule and 
fee proposal are attached he reto and by this reference incorporated herein.  If any 
incorporated term is inconsistent with the Agreement, this Agreement shall control. 
 
2. Consultant’s Expertise.  Consultant warrants that it has the ability, authority, 
capacity and professional expertise to perfo rm this Agreement.  Consultant shall 
provide an adequate staff of experienced  personnel, capable of and devoted to the 
successful accomplishment of the work to be performed hereunder.  Consultant shall 
assign specific individuals to key positio ns.  Once assigned to work under this 
Agreement, key personnel shall not be removed or replaced without District’s prior 
written approval.  
 
3. Independent Contractor.  Consultant acknowledges that  it is an independent 
contractor.  Neither party is or shall purport to be an agent, employee, partner, joint 
venturer or associate of the other.  An employee or agen t of one party shall not be 
deemed an employer or agent  of the other party for any purpose.  Taxes or Social 
Security contributions will not be with held from any paymen t by District, and 
Consultant shall be solely responsible for such matters.

<!-- page 589 -->
Page -2- 
4. Subcontracts.  Consultant shall not enter into any subcontract with respect to any of 
the work to be performed hereunder without District’s prior written approval.  All 
subcontracts shall comply with applicable federal and state laws and regulations and 
shall impose on the subcontractor substantially the same obligations as are imposed 
on Consultant by this Agreement with respect to those matters covered by Sections 8, 
9, 10, 12, 15 and 18.  Consultant is  responsible for full performance of this 
Agreement regardless of whether subcontractors are used. 
 
5. Time for Completion.  Consultant’s services will be provided in a manner consistent 
with the degree of care and skill ordi narily exercised by me mbers of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances.  Consultant’s services 
shall be performed as expeditiously as is consistent with professional skill and care 
and the orderly progress of the Project, and in accord ance with an agreed upon 
schedule for the performance of the Services hereunder.  Time limits established by a 
schedule approved by District shall not, except for reasonable cause, be exceeded by 
District or Consultant.  The Schedule shall be adjusted, if necessary, as the Project 
proceeds.  The date for final performance shall be extended by the number of days 
that performance is delayed by governmental approval or review procedures or other 
causes beyond Consultant’s reasonable control, as jointly confirmed in writing by the 
parties’ representatives. 
 
6. Payment.  District shall pay to Consultant  a not-to-exceed sum of twenty seven 
thousand four hundred dollars  ($27,400.00) as payment in  full for all services 
rendered by Consultant pursuant to this Agreement.  Payments shall be made within 
30 days of District’s receipt of Consultant’s monthly invoices.  Each invoice shall 
detail the work performed during the billing period. 
 
7. Defects in Work.  District may reject any work product that fails to meet customary 
professional standards or Project specifi cations.  Consultant agrees to promptly 
remedy all such deficiencies.  The parties shall make a good faith effort to resolve any 
controversy or claim through informal nego tiation as set forth in Section 12.  No 
compensation shall be paid for any reject ed work until such issues have been 
resolved. 
 
8. Insurance Requirements.   
 
A. Consultant shall maintain in eff ect, at all times during the term of this 
Agreement, insurance adequate to protect District and its agents, 
representatives, officers, officials and employees against such losses as set 
forth below.  Consultant shall provide District with a current Certificate of 
Insurance or a certified copy of the insurance policy naming District as an 
additional insured (except for Errors and Omissions / Professional Liability 
coverage). 
 
 
B. The following types an d amounts of insurance are required as minimums:

           and annual aggregate

           and annual aggregate

occurrence

Standard minimum deductibles are allowed.  Any deductibles are the 
responsibility of Consultant.  Consultant shall immediately inform District of 
any cancellation of insurance or any decrease in the amount of coverage at 
least 30 days before such action takes place.  A violation of this provision may 
be treated as a material breach by Dist rict.  Consultant shall notify District 
upon any termination of its regular prof essional liability coverage and shall 
obtain tail coverage for a minimum of five years from the termination date. 
 
C. Consultant acknowledges that  the amounts of insurance coverage stated above 
shall not limit its liability under this Agreement. 
 
9. Indemnity.  To the fullest extent allowable  by law, Consultant shall defend, 
indemnify and hold harmless District and its representatives, officers, officials and 
employees (the “Indemnitees”) from and against any and all claims, damages, losses 
and expenses (including but not limited to attorney fees, court costs, and the costs of 
appellate proceedings) relating to, arisi ng out of or resulti ng from Consultant’s 
negligent acts, errors, mistakes or omissions in the performance of this Agreement. 
Consultant’s duty to defend, hold harmless and indemnify the Indemnitees shall arise 
in connection with any claim, damage, loss or expense that is attributable to bodily 
injury, sickness, disease, death or injury to, impairment or destruction of property 
including loss of use resulting therefrom, caused in whole or in part by any negligent 
act, error, mistake or omission in the performance of this Agreement (including those 
by any person for whose negligent acts, errors, mistakes or omissions Consultant may 
be liable) to the extent of Consultant’s negligence or fault.  The amount and type of 
insurance coverage requirements set fort h above shall not lim it the scope of the 
indemnity in this paragraph. 
 
10. Records.  Consultant shall retain, and shall require each subcontractor to retain, all 
books, accounts, reports, files and other reco rds relating to this Agreement for a 
period of five years after completion of the work.  All such documents shall be 
subject to inspection and audit by Dist rict upon reasonable notice during normal 
business hours.  A legible co py of any or all such documents shall be produced by 
Consultant at the request and expense of District. 
 
11. Non-Assignment.  Consultant shall not assign any right or interest in this Agreement

<!-- page 591 -->
Page -4- 
without District’s prior written approval, nor shall Consultant delegate any duty 
hereunder without District’s prior written approval. 
 
12. Negotiation of Disputes.  The parties shall make a good faith effort to resolve any 
claim or controversy or claim through informal negotiation.  Notice of any claim or 
controversy shall be provided in writi ng, with supporting documentation, to the 
recipient designated in Section 21.  The recipient shall have seven calendar days to 
prepare and deliver a written response.  If the parties fail to resolve the disputes 
within a reasonable period of not less than ten days, either party may pursue available 
legal remedies. 
 
13. Suspension and Termination by District: 
 
A. Suspension.  District may, without cause, order Consultant in writing to suspend, 
delay or interrupt its performance in whole or part.  An adjustment shall be made 
to the completion date and for any increase in the cost of performance resulting 
from the suspension, delay or interruption.  No adjustment shall be made to the 
extent that performance is or would have been suspended, delayed or interrupted 
by another cause for which Consultant is responsible. 
 
B. Termination for Cause.   District may terminate th is Agreement for cause if 
Consultant refuses or fails to supply enough properly skilled workers to perform 
this Agreement; fails to make required payments to subcontractors; disregards 
laws, ordinances, rules, regulations or  orders of a public  authority having 
jurisdiction; or otherwise commits a material breach of  this Agreement.  When 
any of the foregoing causes exist, District shall give Consultant written notice and 
a ten-day opportunity to cure the default.  If the default remains uncured, District 
may (without prejudice to any other rights or remedies it may have) terminate this 
Agreement and finish the work or cause it to be finished.  If the unpaid balance of 
the Agreement exceeds the cost of fini shing the work (including any expenses 
incurred by District as a re sult of Consultant’s defa ult), Consultant shall be 
entitled to payment for its performance up to the amount of such excess.  If the 
cost of finishing the work exceeds the u npaid balance, Consultant shall pay the 
difference to District. 
 
C. Termination for Convenience.  District, by written notice to Consultant, may 
terminate this Agreement in whole or part when in the sole discretion of District it 
is in District’s best interests to do so.  Consultant sh all be paid for all material, 
equipment and services provided, as well as reasonable termination expenses and 
a reasonable allowance for profit and overhead, provided that such payments, 
exclusive of termination expenses, shal l not exceed the total amount payable 
pursuant to Section 6 less any paymen ts previously made to Consultant.  
Consultant shall not be entitled to profit and overhead on material, equipment and 
services which were not provided. 
 
D. Consultant’s Responsibilities.  Upon receipt of a termination notice, Consultant 
shall (a) promptly discontinue all servic es (unless the notice directs otherwise),

<!-- page 592 -->
Page -5- 
and (b) deliver or otherwise make available to District copies of all data, design 
calculations, drawings, specifications, reports, estimates, summaries and other 
information and materials developed or accumulated by Consultant in performing 
this Agreement. 
 
14. Termination by Consultant.  Consultant may terminate this Agreement upon written 
notice to District if performance is made impossible for a period of 30 consecutive 
calendar days for any of the following reasons through no act or fault of Consultant or 
its agents, employees or subcontractors: issuance of an order by a court or other 
public authority having jurisdiction; an act  of government, such as a declaration of 
national emergency; or a natural disaster or other Act of God.  Consultant may also 
terminate this Agreement upon written notice  and a ten-day opportunity to cure if 
District fails to make any payment within the time set forth in Section 6.  Consultant 
may also terminate this Ag reement upon written notice if  suspensions, delays or 
interruptions by District equal in the aggregate more than 100% of the total number of 
days scheduled for completion. 
 
15. Governing Law.   This Agreement shall  be governed by the law of the State  of 
Arizona.  Any suit arising out of this Agreement shall be brought in the state courts of 
Arizona (with venue in Navajo County) or the federal District of Arizona, but only 
after informal negotiation pursuant to Section 12. 
 
16. Incorporation of Applicable Laws.  Every provision of law required by statute or 
regulation to be in this Agreement will be read and enforced as though included 
herein.  Each party shall promptly notify the other upon discovery that any such 
provision has been omitted. 
 
17. Non-Collusion.  The provisions of A.R.S. § 38-511 are incorporated herein by this 
reference. 
 
18. One-Year Limitation on Actions.  No action shall be maintained by Consultant on 
any claim based upon or aris ing out of this Agreement unless such action is 
commenced within one year after District’s final payment hereunder. 
 
19. Term.  This Contract for Professional Service is valid for one (1) year from the date 
the agreement was made between the two parties. Upon mutual agreement between 
the parties, this contract may be renewed up to four (4) additional one-year terms. 
 
20. Entire Agreement.  This Agreement and those documents incorporated by reference 
represent the entire understanding between  the parties.  No amendment shall be 
effective unless executed by both parties with the same formality as this Agreement.  
Provided, however, that the District’s project manager is authorized to modify the 
Scope of Work, in writing, with the concurrence of Consultant so long as the project 
is not changed substantially or significant additional compensation is not required.

Re: Bourdon Ranch Road / Millet Swale Culverts – Construction Administration and Easements 
WLB No.: 322008A003 
Dear Mr. Flake, 
Thank you for the opportunity to submit our proposal for professional engineering and survey services to 
support the County’s Bourdon Ranch Road / Millet Swale Culverts Project. 
PROJECT UNDERSTANDING: 
Navajo County has recently completed the design of culvert improvements to Bourdon Ranch Road associated 
with the Millet Swale crossing of Bourdon Ranch Road.  The design plans, prepared by The WLB Group, Inc., 
support an increased culvert crossing capacity to help mitigate ongoing impacts created from the upstream 
Millet Swale Reservoir dam breech that occurred in 2021. 
The County will be proceeding with contractor procurement and construction.  WLB has been requested to 
provide construction administration services to support the County through the construction phase.  The 
County has also requested WLB provide several legal descriptions and associated exhibits to obtain drainage 
right-of-way and temporary construction easements from neighboring property owners.  As boundary survey 
was not part of prior County project scopes, the easement will be prepared based upon available record 
information for the adjacent parcels, the roadway, and any monuments found by prior topographic survey 
work in the project area. 
BASIC SERVICES: 
 
TASK 1 – CONSTRUCTION ADMINISTRATION 
WLB will provide Construction Administration servic es during construction.  The following tasks define  the 
typical scope of services that may be provided if/when requested by the County: 
• Pre-Bid Meeting 
• Reponses to Bidder Questions 
• Construction Kickoff Meeting 
• Review of manufacture shop drawings 
• Review of contractor submittals  
• Review of RFI from contractors 
• Review of change orders 
• Review of contractor provided asbuilt/record drawings 
 
Deliverables:  Construction Administration services as requested during construction

<!-- page 595 -->
Will Flake 
Navajo County 
Bourdon Ranch Road / Millet Swale Culverts CA and Easements 
December 19, 2023 
  Page 2 of 3 
   
500 N Beaver Street • Flagstaff, Arizona 86001 • (928) 779-1500 • FAX (928) 779-1501  
TASK 2 – EASEMENT LEGAL DESCRIPTIONS 
WLB will prepare 8 legal descriptions and associate d exhibits, sealed by an Arizona Registered Land Su rveyor, 
for the County’s requested easement areas.  The eas ement dimensions will be based upon coordination an d 
approval of the County.  The service includes the r ecord information research for the adjacent propert ies and 
updating the previously completed plans to depict these easements. 
 
Deliverables: 8 Legal Descriptions and Exhibits; 1 update to the Construction Plans 
 
END OF BASIC SERVICES: 
 
COMPENSATION:   
Our Time and Materials budget for Task 1 – Construction administration is $15,000.  Our Lump Sum Fee for Task 
2 – Easement Legal Descriptions is $12,000.  Our total compensation is $27,400 , including $400 for reimbursable 
expenses. The breakdown of the budget is presented in the following table:  
 
TASK BASIC SERVICES (Time and Materials) BUDGET 
1 Construction Administration $15,000 
Subtotal Time and Materials Services $15,000
